# UnityPy parity inventory

**Status:** P0, 2026-10-28. **Oracle:** UnityPy 1.25.2 with UnityPyBoost, in the oracle image (`DESIGN.md` section 3).

This is the inventory `DESIGN.md` section 3 asks for. It maps every public UnityPy API and behaviour to a tier, a verification level and a test, and gives a per-`TextureFormat` matrix. "Full parity" means every row here is green. It was built by reading UnityPy 1.25.2 as installed in the oracle image, and the claims about what UnityPy does with a texture format come from running it there (section 17).

`tools/parity/inventory.py check` fails when a UnityPy module, class, module-level function, module-level table or setting, class member (its special methods, such as `__eq__`, included), attrs field (the generated classes' included), other class or instance attribute, package re-export, UnityPyBoost export or member, enum member (with its value) or `TextureFormat` is missing from this file, or is named only under a wrong parent (section 21). Section 21 says what it leaves out: private names, special methods aside, and attributes that only code outside a class sets.

## 0. How to read the tables

**Citations.** `file.py:N` is a line of UnityPy 1.25.2, relative to the `UnityPy/` package. `HEAD file.py:N` is UnityPy git `417d595`, the HEAD oracle. Where a table says "lines are in X", a bare `:N` is a line of X.

**Tier** (`DESIGN.md` section 3):

| tier | meaning |
|---|---|
| A | bit-exact with UnityPy wherever UnityPy is right |
| B | UnityPy raises on a valid request; unityrs produces correct output, checked against an independent reference, with a pinned divergence test (section 19) |
| C | UnityPy returns wrong data (checkable against an independent reference), or an exporter or helper of its crashes, or it fails to write; unityrs produces correct output, with a pinned divergence test (section 19). For writing, the row states the acceptance rule |
| Tol | compared within a metric and threshold (Sprite affine path, audio against FMOD), which each Tol row is to state when unityrs's side lands (P1 for the Sprite affine path, P3 for audio); no row states them yet |
| HEAD | equal to UnityPy git `417d595`: to its bundle writer, whose rules a test runs now, and to its dicts on Unity 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures, a target until those fixtures exist (section 20) |
| U | unsupported until a sample and a reference exist, or, for a case its row calls not characterised (no test has run UnityPy on it) where the row has a unityrs side (any tier but —), until a test does and the row takes the tier that run decides: unityrs refuses the input with a named `Unsupported` error. Where UnityPy does not raise (the ETC 3DS formats return pixels nobody has checked), that is a documented divergence, not parity |
| gap | a deliberate `DESIGN.md` gap, and no parity target: UnityPy produces output (through FMOD) and unityrs reports the input unsupported (section 16; `DESIGN.md` section 3, Gap) |
| A, C (or A; C for …) | the item has parts in each tier; the row's notes and section 19 say which. An observable quirk callers can depend on that is not wrong data (a file name, a dict key, a `str()` form) is Tier A |
| — | not a behaviour of its own: a Python helper whose effect the named row covers, or Python-only tooling with no unityrs counterpart. The Python bindings (P3) decide whether to mirror it. A case such a row calls not characterised is not Tier U: unityrs has no side of its own there to refuse it with |

**Level** is a verification level from `DESIGN.md` section 6: L0 detection, L1 container metadata, L2 typetree dicts, L2b TPK nodes, L3 object re-save, L4 file save, L5 pixels, mode and visible hash, L5b PNG bytes, L6 mesh numbers and OBJ text, L7 text exports, L8 audio, L9 extractor tree, L10 consumer shadow runs, L11 3D TRS. **L1 (env)** marks metadata at Environment scope (container paths after the preload pass, cross-file PPtrs): the canonical dump's format 1 leaves it out (`CANONICAL_DUMP.md` section 8), so it needs a format bump. **API** marks a Python-binding behaviour (a return type, an exception, a side effect) with no output of its own; it is tested through `unityrs-py` in P3.

**Status:**
- **verified P0 (Ln):** the oracle (`tools/parity/unitypy_dump.py`) records UnityPy's behaviour at level Ln in the canonical dump, `compare.py` diffs it, and the named self-test pins the oracle's side, or the test column says the corpus dump shows it. The rabex spike, the candidate for the base layer, matched it on every record of the corpus game and UnityPy's samples with its one-hunk header fix (`docs/spikes/rabex.md` section 3). The production crate lands in the phase given after the semicolon.
- **pinned P0:** a test in `tools/parity/tests/` (named in the test column) runs UnityPy on a synthetic input built in code and pins what it does; no corpus is needed, and the canonical dump may not record it. The unityrs side lands in the phase given after the semicolon. The TextureFormat matrix (section 17) is pinned row by row by its own tests.
- A status that names what it covers (`pinned P0 (the modes)`, `verified P0 (L1) for UnityFS`) leaves the rest of the row unverified (source reading).
- **unverified (source reading):** the UnityPy column was written from UnityPy's source, and no test runs it: it is a claim, not a measurement. Where such a row says it was checked in the oracle, that was a run by hand that no test repeats. The planned test in the test column checks unityrs's side, in the phase given after the semicolon.
- **planned (Pn):** the row states no behaviour of UnityPy's: section 17.1's references, whose stated values `test_texture_matrix.py` tier_b_references_hold_their_stated_values checks. unityrs's side lands in phase Pn of `DESIGN.md` section 5.
- **n/a:** tier —. A tier — row whose test column names a test is `pinned P0`, naming what the test covers, with no phase: unityrs has no side of its own there unless the Python bindings (P3) mirror it.

`test_parity_rows.py` every_status_agrees_with_its_test_column holds the statuses to these rules: a pinned or verified row names its test (or the corpus dump) outside a `planned` clause of its test column, an unverified one names none there, `planned` is left to section 17.1, and every row states its own status (no "as above").

**Test** names a self-test in `tools/parity/tests/` (module and test without its `test_` prefix), or says what the planned test compares.

## 1. The oracle stack

The pins are `DESIGN.md` section 3's. The image also carries these libraries, whose versions decide UnityPy's behaviour on the rows named (from `pip list` in `unityrs-dev`, 2026-10-28):

| library | version | decides |
|---|---|---|
| lz4 | 4.4.4 | LZ4/LZ4HC blocks (section 6), `CompressionHelper.compress_lz4` bytes |
| brotli | 1.2.0 | brotli WebFiles |
| pycryptodome | 3.23.0, pinned (`DESIGN.md` section 3) | UnityCN key decryption (`ArchiveStorageManager.decrypt_key`), which without it raises |
| numpy | 2.4.6 | the Sprite affine solve (`SpriteHelper.copy_triangle`), FMOD float-to-int16 conversion |
| pyfmodex | 0.7.2 | FMOD bindings under fmod_toolkit 0.1.3, which ships its own `libfmod` |
| fsspec | 2025.5.1 | `Environment(fs=...)` |
| attrs | 26.1.0 | the generated classes and `ObjectReader` |
| libjpeg-turbo, libwebp, OpenJPEG (bundled with Pillow); zlib (the host's: the wheel links it and bundles no copy) | 2.1.5.1, 1.3.0, 2.5.0, as `tools/parity/pillow_tables.py libraries` prints them; zlib: the host's, as `tools/parity/pillow_tables.py host` prints it | the bytes of the Pillow writers that call them (section 15.8): JPEG, MPO and PDF (`jpeg`); WEBP; JPEG2000 and PDF (`jpeg2k`); ICNS, ICO and PNG (`zip`). The other writers use encoders of Pillow's own (`eps`, `gif`, `pcx`, `raw`). TIFF is one of them: `TiffImagePlugin.WRITE_LIBTIFF` is false, so Pillow writes TIFF with `raw`, and libtiff 4.5.0, also bundled with Pillow, decides no bytes |

zlib is the host's. The Pillow wheel's `_imaging` links `libz.so.1` and the wheel holds no copy of it (no `Pillow.libs/libz-*`), so the zlib behind the PNG, ICO and ICNS bytes is whichever the machine loads, while every other library of the row is the wheel's own. In the oracle image it is zlib 1.2.13 (`tools/parity/pillow_tables.py host`), and that is the reference for those bytes (section 15.8): `tools/parity/oracle_env.py --image` refuses an interpreter that loads another (section 21). CI's venv holds the same wheels and loads its runner's zlib. `test_pillow_tables.py` libraries_row_is_what_the_writers_call holds the row to what the wheel decides, whatever the host's zlib, and checks that the process loads each bundled library from `Pillow.libs` and zlib from elsewhere.

TypeTreeGeneratorAPI is not installed, so `TypeTreeGenerator` raises `ImportError` in the oracle (section 10).

### 2.1 Package re-exports

What a package's `__init__.py` makes reachable from `import UnityPy` (`inventory.py` group `exports`). Each name is the same object as the class, function or table whose own row gives its tier; this table is its import path, which the Python bindings mirror. `UnityPy.helpers` and `UnityPy.export` re-export only their own submodules (their `__all__`; sections 22 and 15), and `export/__init__.py` leaves `ShaderConverter` out. `resources` and `tools` re-export nothing.

Every row is tier A at level API. `test_parity_rows.py` package_aliases_and_reexports imports each package and finds every member listed, so the table is pinned P0; the binding test is planned, and the binding lands in P3.

## 3. Environment and loading

Lines are in `environment.py`.

**The walk.** `load_folder` and `find_file` walk a folder with `fs.walk` (fsspec 2025.5.1's `AbstractFileSystem.walk` over `LocalFileSystem.ls`, for the default local `fs`). It lists each folder's entries in `os.scandir` order, to which nothing applies a sort: the order the file system returns, which differs between file systems and between two folders holding the same names. It yields the folder's files in that order, then walks each of its subfolders in that same order, depth-first, so all of a folder's files come before any file of its subfolders. `Environment.files` after `load_folder`, `Environment.objects` in turn, the registry's entry among same-named files (the last one loaded) and `find_file`'s first match all follow this order; a sorted walk, or one that enters a subfolder where the listing meets it, gives others. Five more of its rules decide what it yields:
- **Absolute posix paths.** `walk` first strips the folder with `LocalFileSystem._strip_protocol`, whose `make_path_posix` joins a relative folder to the cwd, drops a leading `./` and any trailing `/`, expands a leading `~` (a `~name` the home lookup cannot expand stays as it is, relative, so `load_folder("~nouser")` keys `~nouser/t.bin`), and keeps `..`, so it yields `/cwd/rel/a.bin` for `rel`, `./rel` and `rel/`, and `/cwd/rel/../rel/a.bin` for `rel/../rel` (fsspec `implementations/local.py`, `_strip_protocol` and `make_path_posix`). `load_folder` keys `files` by those paths, and `find_file`'s walk lists them, while `Environment.path` keeps the folder as given: `Environment("rel")` has `path` `rel` and keys `/cwd/rel/a.bin`.
- **Links under the folder are files.** `LocalFileSystem.ls` calls an entry a directory only when `os.DirEntry.is_dir(follow_symlinks=False)` says so, and the walk takes every other entry for a file (fsspec `implementations/local.py`, `info`; `spec.py`, `walk`). A link to a folder is listed as a file and never entered: `load_folder` opens it and raises `IsADirectoryError`, and `find_file`'s walk reaches no file under it (only its `path/name` step, given the link's name, does). A broken link is listed too: `load_folder` opens it and raises `FileNotFoundError`, while `find_file` returns `None` for one its walk matches (its row). A link to a file loads under the link's path, and registers its CAB under the link's name.
- **A folder it cannot list yields nothing.** `walk` runs with `on_error="omit"`, so a folder `ls` raises for is skipped with no error: `load_folder` of a missing folder loads nothing, and a subfolder without read permission is left out while its siblings load. A path that is a file walks as a listing of itself under the name `""`, so `load_folder` of a file opens `path/` and raises `NotADirectoryError`.
- **The folder given is followed.** `ls` tests the folder itself with `info`, which stats a link's target for a path string (`os.stat(follow_symlinks=True)`; fsspec `implementations/local.py`, `info`), so the rule for links holds for the entries only. A link given as the folder walks its target under the link's own path: `load_folder("link")` keys `/cwd/link/c.bin`, and `find_file`'s walk of a `path` that is a link lists those paths. A broken link given as the folder yields nothing, with no error, and a link to a file walks as that file does, so `load_folder` opens `link/` and raises `NotADirectoryError`. The constructor's `isdir` and `isfile` tests follow a link too (the constructor's row).
- **URL-style prefixes.** `_strip_protocol` removes a leading `file://`, `file:`, `local://` or `local:` before `make_path_posix` (fsspec `implementations/local.py`, `_strip_protocol`), so every call that reaches the disk through `fs` takes a local path so prefixed: the constructor's `isfile` and `isdir`, `load_folder`'s and `find_file`'s walks, `find_file`'s `exists`, `load`, `load_zip_file`, and `_load_split_file`, which tests and opens each part (`:89-98`). `load_file`'s `os.path` tests and `find_sensitive_path`'s `os.listdir` do not (`:117-133`). So a folder or a zip so named loads: `Environment("file:rel")` keeps `path` `file:rel` and keys `/cwd/rel/a.bin`, as `load_folder("file:rel")` does, and `Environment("file:x.zip")` and `load_zip_file("file:x.zip")` load its members. So does a `.splitN` name, which `load_file` and `load_assets` hand to `_load_split_file` before any `os.path` test (`:111-116`, `:296-304`): it is keyed by its prefixed base path and registered as a CAB under that path's simplified name, so `Environment("file:/d/a.assets.split0")` sets `path` to `file:/d` and keys `file:/d/a.assets`, CAB `a.assets`, and `Environment("file:b.assets.split0")`, in the cwd, keys and registers `file:b.assets` (`ntpath.basename` does not split at `file:`). Any other prefixed name does not load through `load_file`, which misses it, and returns `None` where `os.listdir` can list `Environment.path` (the cwd by default); where `Environment.path` has a prefix it cannot, and `load_file` raises `FileNotFoundError` for any name `os.path` does not find. So the constructor, which sets `path` to a file argument's `ntpath.dirname`, `.` when that is empty, loads nothing with no error for such an argument with no folder part (`file:x.bin`, `local:x.bin`), and raises `FileNotFoundError` naming that prefixed folder for one with a folder part, wherever the file lies (`file:./x.bin`, whose `path` is `file:.`; `file:/d/x.bin`, whose `path` is `file:/d`; `file://x.bin`, whose `path` is `file:`); and `find_file` from a prefixed `Environment.path` raises it where its `path/name` step finds the file, a `.splitN` name aside, which loads through the split branch as above (`find_file("a.assets.split0")` from `path` `file:/d` keys `file:/d/a.assets`, CAB `a.assets`), while a file only its walk finds loads. Tier B where UnityPy raises, C where it loads nothing (section 19.2).

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Environment` constructor | each argument: a path that is a file with extension `.apk`/`.zip` (case-sensitive) goes to `load_zip_file`, which keys its members by their own names and leaves `path` alone; another file (`.ZIP` included) sets `path` to its `ntpath.dirname`, `.` where that is empty (a bare name), so a backslash or a drive colon splits a posix name too (`a\b.assets` sets `a`, `C:c.assets` sets `C:`), and loads it (a `.splitN` name, which `reSplit` matches at its start, through `load_files`: the `.splitN` merging row), so with several files the last one's directory wins; a directory sets `path` and loads it recursively through `load_folder` (a zip in it goes through detection, section 4: it is a raw file unless its fifth byte is NUL, and then its members load and the zip's own key holds `None`, the `load_files` row); a string that is neither is ignored without an error. Those tests follow a link: a link to a folder is walked as that folder, under the link's path, a link to a file loads under the link's path, and a broken link is neither (section 3, the folder given). A local path with a URL-style prefix passes them, and then a folder, a zip or a `.splitN` name loads, while another file loads nothing where the argument has no folder part (`file:x.bin`) and raises `FileNotFoundError` where it has one (section 3, URL-style prefixes). Any other argument goes to `load_file` (next rows): a stream with a `name` attribute is keyed by that name and registered as a CAB under its simplified name (the lower-cased basename), so a `PPtr` to that CAB resolves. That holds for a `str` name, as the builtin `open` of a `str` path gives. A stream whose `name` is not a `str` is keyed by that value, and a bundle or WebFile loads under it, with `File.name` `""` (section 5.2): `tempfile.TemporaryFile()`'s file descriptor (an `int`: `files[3]`), `SpooledTemporaryFile`'s `None`, or the `bytes` path of a stream opened from one (`open(os.fsencode(p), "rb")`). A SerializedFile raises from `name.endswith` given `str` suffixes (`helpers/ImportHelper.py:138`): `AttributeError` for an `int` or `None` ("'int' object has no attribute 'endswith'"), `TypeError` for `bytes` ("a bytes-like object is required, not 'str'"). A raw file with an `int` or `None` name raises `TypeError` ("expected str, bytes or os.PathLike object, not int") registering its CAB (`:263`, `:363`); with a `bytes` name it loads, registered as a CAB under its `bytes` basename lower-cased (`b'raw.bin'`), since `ntpath.basename` takes `bytes`. With `name=` all of them load (section 19.2). Anything else is keyed by `str(obj.__hash__())`: for `bytes` and a read-only `memoryview` the bytes' string hash, which `PYTHONHASHSEED` randomises per process, and for a nameless stream or an `EndianBinaryReader` its id-based hash, so two equal streams get two keys (section 19.2). A `bytearray`, which UnityPy's own `FileSourceType` lists, raises `TypeError`: the default key is evaluated eagerly and calls its `__hash__`, which is `None`. A writable `memoryview` raises `ValueError` "cannot hash writable memoryview object" the same way. With `name=` both load (section 19.2). A `pathlib.Path` raises `TypeError` "Unsupported type for EndianBinaryReader" (`:54-70`, `:141-149`, `helpers/ImportHelper.py:13`). An object with `read` but no `seek` or `tell` raises `TypeError` "memoryview: a bytes-like object is required, not 'ReadOnly'", `ReadOnly` being its class (section 13, `EndianBinaryReader`; section 19.2), while an `EndianBinaryReader` of either kind is taken as it is and loads. `path` defaults to the cwd for a local fs, and to `''` for any other (`:46-52`). Through a non-local `fs` the row states only the cases its test runs: a `MemoryFileSystem` holds each argument, at an absolute path under a top-level folder the local disk lacks unless said otherwise (`Environment.fs`). There `a.assets.split0` and `a.assets.split1` load, their parts read through `fs`, and so does a folder argument holding only those two parts; `z.zip` and `z.apk` load their members; `x.assets` raises `FileNotFoundError` naming its folder, and loads nothing, with no error, where its folder is instead an empty one the local disk can list; a folder argument holding `z.zip`, or the two parts and `sub/x.assets`, raises `FileNotFoundError` naming that file. Every other constructor argument through a non-local `fs` is not characterised (Tier U). | `ImportHelper.check_file_type` | under 20 bytes `ResourceFile`; signature to NUL within 20 bytes, read with the reader the input gets (section 13): from bytes, an input of exactly 20 bytes with no NUL raises `Exception` "String not terminated" (the memory reader reaches the end), while a longer one takes its first 20 bytes as the signature and detection goes on (21, 127 and 200 bytes of `A` are `ResourceFile`s), and from a path or a stream 20 bytes with no NUL read as a name and the file is a `ResourceFile`; bundle, web and `PK\x03\x04` signatures; under 128 bytes `ResourceFile`; gzip and brotli WebFiles; else a big-endian SerializedFile header sanity check (`:44-124`) | A | L0 | `test_dump.py` l0_detection_and_name_rules | verified P0 (L0); impl P1. | zip detection | only when the fifth byte is NUL, which ends the `PK\x03\x04` signature (`:69-70`): the low byte of "version needed to extract", which is 20 in what Python's `zipfile` writes, so such a zip is a `ResourceFile` | A | L0 | `test_dump.py` l0_detection_and_name_rules; `test_rows_paths.py` a_zip_loads_from_bytes_and_raises_from_a_path | verified P0 (L0); impl P3 |
| gzip WebFile detection | decompresses the whole stream; a bad stream raises (`:77-83`). It reads through the shared reader and returns without putting it back to 0, so `WebFile` then reads its two-byte magic past the start, gets nothing, takes the file for plain, and raises `ValueError` "Invalid WebFile signature" on the gzip bytes (`files/WebFile.py:20-21`, `:40-42`): no gzip WebFile loads, from a path, from bytes or as a bundle entry. One that moves it before the file's start, as -100 does, raises `ValueError` "Position cannot be negative" from a path. ### 5.2 WebFile, File and resources

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WebFile` | gzip by magic (never reached through loading, which has moved the reader first: section 4, gzip WebFile detection), brotli by `brotli` at 0x20, else plain (`files/WebFile.py:20-37`); entry offsets and sizes are signed `int`s not checked against the data (`:50-51`), and an entry past the end reads short (section 13); they are applied by the file's own reader, the memory reader of the decompressed bytes for a brotli file from any source (`:31-34`), so from bytes, or for a brotli file, a negative offset counts from the file's end and a negative size reads a slice, while for a plain file from a path, read by the stream reader, a negative offset raises `ValueError` "Position cannot be negative" and a size below -1 `ValueError` "read length must be non-negative or -1" (section 13, negative sizes and positions); signature must start `UnityWebData` or `TuanjieWebData` (`:40-43`); entry names decoded as strict UTF-8 (`:53`). An entry's name length is a signed `int` too, and the same reader reads the name with it (`:52-53`): -1 reads the rest of the data as the name, from bytes and from a path, and the entry keeps its offset and size; below -1, for a plain file from a path the stream reader raises `ValueError` "read length must be non-negative or -1", and from bytes, or for a brotli file from a path, the memory reader reads the slice `view[P:P + n]` and moves back by the length (section 13), into the header it has read, which the loop reads again: where that lands on an entry's start, as -12 does on the entry being read (its offset, size and name length are its first 12 bytes), it adds that entry, with the name `""`, over and over until memory runs out. The header loop runs while `Position` is below the header length (`:46-54`), so where that length lies past the data the reader decides (section 13, reads past the end): for a plain 57-byte file with a header length of 1000 and one entry whose name length is 2000 (the case tested), from bytes the memory reader's `Position` passes 1000 and the entry loads, named by the rest of the data, while from a path or an `io.BytesIO` the stream reader's stops at 57, the loop reads on, and `struct.error` "unpack requires a buffer of 4 bytes" escapes. The dump records it as `kind: web`. | A; B for gzip; U for an entry name length below -1 | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` brotli_webfile_loads; the gzip failure: `test_rows_containers.py` gzip_webfile_is_detected_and_never_parsed; negative offsets, sizes and name lengths: `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data; a header length past the data: `test_rows_loading.py` the_stream_reader_stops_at_the_end; the rest: planned WebFile fixtures | pinned P0 (brotli, the gzip failure, negative offsets, sizes and name lengths, a header length past the data); impl P3 |

| `File.get_assets` | a generator of the SerializedFile children, and those of nested BundleFiles and WebFiles, depth-first in `files` order; raw readers are skipped. On a SerializedFile it yields nothing: its `return self` sits in a generator, so it ends it (`files/File.py:41-50`) | A; C on a SerializedFile (section 19.2) | API | `test_rows_containers.py` file_iteration_and_dict_access | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `File.get_objects` | a generator over `files` (`files/File.py:65-74`): a SerializedFile child gives its objects; a raw-reader child is an `EndianBinaryReader`, not an `ObjectReader`, and is skipped; an `ObjectReader` value is yielded itself, which is what a SerializedFile's own `files` holds (its `objects`, `files/SerializedFile.py:225-233`), so on a SerializedFile it yields its objects. A BundleFile or WebFile child is iterated as `f.objects`, which neither class has, so a container holding one (a WebGL `.data` file holding a bundle) raises `AttributeError` | A; B for a nested container (section 19.2) | API | `test_rows_containers.py` file_iteration_and_dict_access | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `File.get_filtered_objects` | with types, the objects of each SerializedFile child whose `obj.type` is one of them; a nested container raises as in `get_objects`, and a SerializedFile yields nothing, because the `ObjectReader`s its `files` holds take neither branch. With `None` or `[]`, nothing: its `return self.get_objects()` sits in a generator, so it ends it (`files/File.py:52-63`) | A; B for a nested container; C with no types and on a SerializedFile (section 19.2) | API | `test_rows_containers.py` file_iteration_and_dict_access | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `File.container` | dict union of the containers of the children that are `File`s, path to asset pointer, a later path replacing an earlier one (`files/File.py:123-125`) | A | L1 (env) | `test_rows_environment.py` split_files_folders_and_save; planned: env dump | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| `File.get`, `File.keys`, `File.items`, `File.values` | `get(key, default)` is `getattr(self, key, default)`, an attribute lookup, not a lookup in `files`: `bundle.get("CAB-…")` is `None`, and `bundle.get("files")` is the dict. `keys`, `items`, `values` and `[]` (`File.__getitem__`) are those of `files` (`files/File.py:127-143`), which on a SerializedFile is its `objects` | A | API | `test_rows_containers.py` file_iteration_and_dict_access | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `File.mark_changed` | marks the file and every parent that is a `File`, not the Environment (`files/File.py:145-148`) | A | L4 | `test_rows_environment.py` split_files_folders_and_save; planned: save suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `File.get_writeable_cab` | section 18 | | | | |
| `ResourceReader.get_resource_data` | looks up the CAB by basename, then `{name}.resource`, `{name}.assets.resS`, `{name}.resS`, in the Environment's one registry (`Environment.register_cab`), not in the asking file's own bundle, so when two bundles hold a CAB of the same name the later one's `.resS` supplies the data for both (section 19.2); on a miss calls `SerializedFile.load_dependencies` with those names (which loads the file's externals as non-dependencies, section 7.2) and looks again; raises `FileNotFoundError` (`helpers/ResourceReader.py:10-32`). `load_dependencies` swallows only `FileNotFoundError` (`files/SerializedFile.py:345-349`), so a name the loader cannot open otherwise escapes: with no CAB `""` registered, an empty name is joined to `Environment.path`, a directory, and raises `IsADirectoryError`. Once a zip with a directory entry has registered CAB `""` (section 3, `Environment.load_zip_file`), the lookup finds that empty reader first, and an empty name reads `b""` | A; C for same-named CABs | L5 | `test_rows_environment.py` same_named_cabs_share_one_registry_entry; `test_rows_textures.py` an_empty_texture_follows_an_empty_stream_path (the names in order, the miss, the empty name); planned: `.resS` texture fixture | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| `.resS` read | `reader.Position = offset; read_bytes(size)` on the CAB (`helpers/ResourceReader.py:36-38`); a range past the end returns the bytes that exist, with no error (section 13) | A | L5 | `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data; planned: `.resS` texture fixture | pinned P0; impl P1 |

## 6. Compression codecs

Lines are in `helpers/CompressionHelper.py`. The bundle codec is `flags & 0x3F` as `CompressionFlags` (`enums/BundleFile.py:4-9`).

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NONE (0) | identity (`:239`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` bundle_layouts_names_and_child_failures | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| LZMA (1) | 5-byte props, raw LZMA1, no size check: UnityFS ignores the stored size (`:17-44`, `:240`). See `docs/spikes/lzma.md` for truncated and over-long blocks. | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples; `docs/spikes/lzma.md` fixtures | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| LZ4 (2), LZ4HC (3) | `lz4.block.decompress(data, stored_size)` (`:83-94`, `:241-242`): the stored size is the buffer's capacity, not a check. Output shorter than it is kept as is, with no error, and every later entry offset shifts; output that does not fit raises `LZ4BlockError`, so a stored size of 0 does for any non-empty output. The same holds for an LZ4 blocks-info (`files/BundleFile.py:141`). An empty block, `b"\x00"` (what `lz4.block.compress(b"")` writes), stored as 0 decodes to `b""`, and the bundle loads | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples; `test_rows_containers.py` lz4_stored_size_is_a_capacity | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| LZHAM (4) | raises `NotImplementedError` (`:234-235`, `:243`) | U | L1 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: error record | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| other codec values | raise `ValueError` "Unknown compression" (`files/BundleFile.py:515-521`) | A | L1 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: error record | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| gzip | `gzip.decompress` (`:133-142`); WebFile only. Detection already decompresses the whole stream, and leaves the reader where it stopped, so no gzip WebFile loads (section 4); unityrs loads them, checked against `gzip.decompress` and the plain WebFile layout (section 19.2) | B | L0, L1 | `test_dump.py` l0_detection_and_name_rules (L0); `test_rows_containers.py` gzip_webfile_is_detected_and_never_parsed; WebFile fixture (L1) | verified P0 (L0); impl P3 |
| brotli | `brotli.decompress` (`:109-118`); WebFile only. Detection takes any file of 128 bytes or more with `brotli` at offset 0x20 for a WebFile, whatever it holds (`helpers/ImportHelper.py:86-90`): the metadata block Unity's brotli builds open with puts it there. A brotli stream without it loads as a raw file, and one with it that holds no WebFile raises one of three errors: `brotli.error` for a stream that does not decode; for content with no NUL in reach, a plain `Exception` "String not terminated" when the content is at most 32767 bytes (the memory reader reaches its end, section 13: `read_string_to_null` raises on 32767 bytes with no NUL and returns 32767 characters from 32768); and otherwise `ValueError` "Invalid WebFile signature" (a NUL-free longer content gives a 32767-byte signature) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` brotli_webfile_loads | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.decompress_lzma` | `read_decompressed_size` skips an 8-byte size, for UnityWeb (`:43`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples (UnityFS); UnityWeb: planned fixture | verified P0 (L1) for UnityFS; impl P1 |
| `CompressionHelper.decompress_lz4` | as LZ4 above | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| `CompressionHelper.decompress_brotli`, `CompressionHelper.decompress_gzip` | `brotli.decompress` and `gzip.decompress` (`:109-118`, `:133-142`) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` brotli_webfile_loads; `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.decompress_lzham` | as LZHAM above | U | L1 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: error record | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| `CompressionHelper.compress_lzma` | props `0x5D`, dictionary 8 MiB, lc 3 lp 0 pb 2, BT4, nice_len 123, optional u64 size (`:47-79`) | A | L4 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: save suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.compress_lz4` | `lz4.block.compress` HC level 9, no size prefix (`:97-105`); used for LZ4 and LZ4HC | A | L4 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: save suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.compress_gzip`, `CompressionHelper.compress_brotli` | library defaults (`:121-155`): brotli at quality 11, and gzip at level 9 with the current time, in whole seconds, as the header's mtime, so two gzip saves of one file made in different seconds differ | A | L4 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: save suite (after decompression) | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.DECOMPRESSION_MAP`, `CompressionHelper.COMPRESSION_MAP`, `CompressionHelper.COMPRESSION_CHUNK_SIZE_MAP` | the codec tables by `CompressionFlags` (`:238-258`): LZ4HC reads and writes as LZ4 HC, chunks are 128 KiB for LZ4 and LZ4HC and `0xFFFFFFFF` otherwise. They are looked up on every block (`files/BundleFile.py:515-518`), so adding an entry is how a UnityPy user registers a custom codec; unityrs's codec registry is the counterpart (`DESIGN.md` section 4) | A | API | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: codec-registry binding | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.GZIP_MAGIC`, `CompressionHelper.BROTLI_MAGIC` | `1f 8b` at offset 0 and `brotli` at `0x20`, the WebFile sniffs (`:12-13`, `files/WebFile.py:20-37`); through loading the gzip sniff never matches, because detection has moved the reader (section 4) | A | L1 | `test_rows_containers.py` webfile_sniffs_its_codec; planned: WebFile fixtures | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `CompressionHelper.chunk_based_compress` | LZ4/LZ4HC in 128 KiB chunks, LZMA in chunks of 0xFFFFFFFF bytes (one for a payload under 4 GiB); `none` always one block, of size 0 for an empty payload, while a compressing codec writes no block for an empty payload; a chunk whose compressed form is larger than it, and only such a chunk, is stored as it is, with its codec bits cleared (one that compresses to exactly its own size stays compressed); a codec with no compressor (LZHAM) raises `NotImplementedError` (`:158-231`, sizes `:253-258`) | A | L4 | `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers; planned: save suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |

## 7. SerializedFile

### 7.1 Version gates

Every branch on `header.version`. Lines are in `files/SerializedFile.py` unless named. The canonical dump records each one at L1 (`CANONICAL_DUMP.md` section 4.2). P0 exercises format 17 (UnityPy's samples), format 22 (the corpus game) and the synthetic formats 1 to 22 that `tests/synth.py` builds: `test_rows_files.py` old_format_gates loads formats 1 to 15 in both endians. P4's Editor-built fixtures check the same gates on files Unity wrote.

Any other string goes to `UnityVersion.from_str` with no fallback, so one its pattern does not match raises `ValueError` "Invalid version string: 2017.4" (`2017.4` and `abc`, at formats 9, 15 and 22, the cases tested, with `config.FALLBACK_UNITY_VERSION` set or not), and the file does not load: `load_file` and `UnityPy.load` raise it | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` version_strings_pack_as_unitypy_packs_them; `test_rows_internals.py` an_unmatched_version_string_raises | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| AssetBundle at load | the first AssetBundle object is parsed while loading and builds the container, and no other object is read; a broken one fails the file. So does one whose format-22 data offset puts it before the file's start, from a path (`ValueError` "Position cannot be negative") and from bytes where the slice the memory reader reads is empty (`EOFError`); from bytes a slice that ends before index 0 is counted from the file's end, and one that holds the AssetBundle's bytes loads (section 8, `ObjectReader.from_reader`). A second AssetBundle object is not read (`files/SerializedFile.py:316-324`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples (container); the failing path: `test_rows_objects.py` object_container | verified P0 (L1); impl P1. The failing path: pinned P0 |
| `SerializedFile.container` | the file's `ContainerHelper` (`files/SerializedFile.py:326-328`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` samples | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| `config.SERIALIZED_FILE_PARSE_TYPETREE` | `False` turns `_enable_type_tree` off only after the types and ref types, trees included, were read (`files/SerializedFile.py:284`, `:308`, `:310-311`, `config.py:12`), so despite its docstring it saves no load time and reading is unchanged (`ObjectReader` still uses each type's tree, `files/ObjectReader.py:296-297`); its one effect is on `SerializedFile.save`, which then writes no trees and, from format 13, the tree flag as false (`files/SerializedFile.py:179`, `:405`). From 13 the saved file reloads through the TPK; below 13 the format has no flag and its reader expects the trees, so UnityPy does not read the file back (section 19.2) | A; C (writing) below format 13 (section 19.2) | L4 | `test_rows_writing.py` serialized_file_save; planned: config suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |

### 9.2 Object mode

Object mode has two builders. `ObjectReader` passes its `byte_size` (section 8), and `TypeTreeHelper.read_typetree` takes UnityPyBoost's path whenever that is non-zero (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:146`), so with UnityPyBoost installed, as in the oracle, every object-mode parse of a loaded object that holds data goes through UnityPyBoost's `parse_class` (`UnityPyBoost/TypeTreeHelper.cpp:458-583`, called at `:872`): that is the oracle's rule, and unityrs's. The pure-Python path (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:233-258`) runs for an object of zero bytes, for a direct `TypeTreeHelper.read_typetree` call without `byte_size`, and without UnityPyBoost, and builds differently.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| class lookup | the node's `m_Type` looked up on `UnityPy.classes`; a `PPtr<...>` node becomes a `PPtr` holding the file as `assetsfile`. UnityPyBoost builds a type the module lacks as `UnknownObject(__node__=node, **values)` (`UnityPyBoost/TypeTreeHelper.cpp:476-499`); the pure-Python path calls `getattr(classes, m_Type, UnknownObject)(**values)` (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:233-242`), so its `UnknownObject` has `__node__` `None` and `get_type()` returns `None`. The lookup is `getattr` on the module, so a type that names one of its attributes that is not a class (a submodule such as `math`, a typing form such as `List`, a dunder such as `__name__`), or an attribute of the module's type, which `dir` does not list (`__repr__`, or `__class__`, the module type), gets that object, which UnityPyBoost then calls; section 9.2.1's second table gives the result for each. For most of them the call fails, the object has no `__slots__`, and `parse_class` reads the `NULL` that `PyObject_GetAttrString` returns as a tuple (`:508-509`), so the process dies of `SIGSEGV` | A; B and C for a type that names anything else `getattr` finds on the module (section 19.2) | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` object_mode_builds_classes_as_parse_class_does; `test_object_mode.py` table_is_what_parse_class_does; planned: object-mode suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| attribute names | each child node's `_clean_name` (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:231`), which UnityPyBoost computes with its C++ rule: only an ASCII digit gets the `x` prefix (`UnityPyBoost/TypeTreeHelper.cpp:59`), so a field `²ab` is the attribute `²ab` and `2cd` is `x2cd` | A | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` object_mode_names_use_the_boost_rule; planned: object-mode suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| construction (UnityPyBoost) | `parse_class`: 1. `clz(**values)`, and any exception is cleared (`:501-506`). 2. When `clz.__slots__`, read with `getattr` and so found through the MRO, is a non-empty tuple, the result is `UnknownObject(__node__=node, **values)` (`:508-515`). Which classes have one is section 9.2.1's table, built from what `parse_class` does with every class of the module, not from the bases: attrs gives a class it builds with `slots=True` (a generated class whose first base is `object`, `Object` or `ABC`, `classes/generated.py:38-39`) its own fields as `__slots__`, and adds `__weakref__` when that base is `object` or `ABC`, which have no weak-reference slot, so such a class with no fields on `Object` (`GameManager`, `EmptyObject`, `Derived`) has `()`, as has every class attrs builds with `slots=False` (`TextAsset`, `Texture2D`), which finds ABC's `()`. 3. Otherwise the values whose name is not in `clz.__annotations__` (the class's own annotations; a generated class declares every field itself) are taken out as extras (`:519-536`). With none, the result is `UnknownObject(__node__=node, **values)`. Else `clz(**rest)`, then a generic attribute set of each extra; if either fails, `UnknownObject(__node__=node, **values)` (`:538-574`): the set fails where instances have no `__dict__` (`ABC`). So a missing optional field takes its default; a missing required field gives an `UnknownObject`; and an extra field gives an `UnknownObject` for a class whose `__slots__` is a non-empty tuple or whose instances have no `__dict__`, and the class with the extra as an attribute for every other (section 9.2.1 lists both). A MonoBehaviour field, or `[SerializeReference]` data, whose type shares the name of a class of the first kind and has fields that class lacks therefore comes out as an `UnknownObject`, and one whose type shares the name of a class of the second kind (`GameManager`, `Derived`) as that class | A | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` object_mode_builds_classes_as_parse_class_does; `test_object_mode.py` table_is_what_parse_class_does; `test_object_mode.py` an_extra_field_follows_slots_and_dict_for_every_class; `test_object_mode.py` why_a_generated_class_has_its_slots; planned: object-mode suite, with a corpus MonoBehaviour field whose script type shares a generated class's name | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| construction (pure-Python path) | `clz(**values)`; only a `TypeError` is caught (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:242-258`). Then: if any annotation key is absent, an optional one included, or the class is `UnknownObject`, the result is `UnknownObject(node, **values)`; else, with extras, `clz(**annotated)` and a `setattr` of each extra, which raises `AttributeError` out of the read on a class whose instances have no `__dict__` (section 9.2.1: `AssetDatabaseMetrics`, a math record, `ABC`) and succeeds on one with a non-empty `__slots__` and the `__dict__` `Object` gives (`MarshallingTestObject`); without extras, `UnknownObject(**values)` with no node. So it differs from UnityPyBoost in three cases: an optional field missing beside an extra (`UnknownObject` here, the class there); an extra on a class whose `__slots__` is a non-empty tuple or whose instances have no `__dict__` (`AttributeError`, or the class with the extra, where UnityPyBoost gives an `UnknownObject`); and a type the module lacks (no node). `typing.TypeVar`, which the module imports, raises `TypeError` here (section 9.2.1) | A; C for the `AttributeError` (section 19.2) | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` object_mode_builds_classes_as_parse_class_does; `test_object_mode.py` table_is_what_parse_class_does (`TypeVar`'s `TypeError`, section 9.2.1) | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| pure-Python class arrays | a vector whose element node is aligned (meta flag `0x4000`) is read by `read_value_array`, whose class rule differs from a single class's (previous row): it checks the annotations before it builds, so a missing annotation, an optional one included, gives `UnknownObject(node, **values)` where `clz(**values)` would build; a type the module lacks keeps its node (`get_type()` is the type, where a single class drops it); extras are set with `setattr`, `AttributeError` without a `__dict__`. An unaligned element is read by `read_value`, with the single-class rules (`helpers/TypeTreeHelper.py:211-216`, `:314-357`) | A | API | `test_rows_objects.py` pure_python_class_arrays; planned: object-mode suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |

### 9.2.1 By class

What object mode builds for every name `getattr` finds on `UnityPy.classes`, the module a class node's type is looked up on (`PyObject_GetAttr`, `UnityPyBoost/TypeTreeHelper.cpp:488`): the module's own attributes, which `dir(UnityPy.classes)` lists, and the attributes of the module's type, which it leaves out (`__class__`, `__repr__`, `__hash__`, ...). The module defines no `__getattr__`, so there are no others. The tables are what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/object_mode.py` prints in the oracle image, and `test_object_mode.py` table_is_what_parse_class_does requires this section to hold exactly that.

The first table takes each class: a tree of its type holding every attrs field the class has and one more, `m_Extra`, read on UnityPyBoost's path and on the pure-Python path. "The class, `m_Extra` an attribute" means the class was built and the extra set on it; `UnknownObject` is the fallback, carrying the node. The `__slots__` column is the value step 2 of `parse_class` reads (section 9.2), and the `__dict__` column decides whether step 3's attribute set succeeds. An alias is listed under its own name (`float3` is `Vector3f`). `test_object_mode.py` an_extra_field_follows_slots_and_dict_for_every_class requires every row to follow section 9.2's rule: an `UnknownObject` on UnityPyBoost exactly where `__slots__` is a non-empty tuple or there is no `__dict__`.

The second table takes every other name: the module's attributes that are not classes, and the attributes of the module's type, `__class__` among them, which is a class (the module type) but not one of the module's, and has no `__slots__`. Each is a class node of that type holding no field, and holding one, nested in a holder so that UnityPyBoost reads it, each in a process of its own. unityrs builds every one of them as a type the module lacks (section 19.2).

### 9.3 TypeTreeNode

Lines are in `helpers/TypeTreeNode.py`.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TypeTreeNode` | node with `m_Level`, `m_Type`, `m_Name`, `m_ByteSize`, `m_Version`, `m_Children`, `m_TypeFlags`, `m_VariableCount`, `m_Index`, `m_MetaFlag`, `m_RefTypeHash` (`:52-64`, `:70`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` tables_nodes_and_entries_follow_unitypy | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| `TypeTreeNode.parse` | old format, stack-based, version gates of section 7.1 (`:78-109`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` tables_nodes_and_entries_follow_unitypy | verified P0 (L1); impl P4 |
| `TypeTreeNode.parse_blob` | blob rows under a hidden root; only the root's first child is kept; offsets with the high bit index the TPK's whole common-string table, and an offset missing from it becomes its decimal text (`:111-154`, `:129`). The node count and the string-buffer size are signed and unchecked, and each sizes a `read` by section 13's rules (`:113-118`): a negative count reads a slice that `struct.iter_unpack` refuses with `struct.error` unless its length is a multiple of the node size, a count of 0 leaves the root with no child and raises `IndexError`, and a string-buffer size of -1 reads the rest of the data as the buffer | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` type_indices_and_blob_trees_follow_python; the signed count and size: `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data | verified P0 (L1); impl P1. The signed count and size: pinned P0 |
| `TypeTreeNode.from_list` | builds a tree from node dicts or nodes (`:156-188`). For dicts it needs `m_Level`, `m_Type` and `m_Name` in the first, and decides the defaults from the first alone: when the first lacks `m_ByteSize` or `m_Version`, every dict gets 0 for it (and one that has it raises `TypeError` for a repeated argument); when the first has it, a later dict without it raises `TypeError` (`:164-172`) | A | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` tpk_version_lookup_and_from_list; planned: provider suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.traverse` | pre-order (`:71-76`) | A | API | `test_rows_objects.py` typetree_node_dumps; planned: binding | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.get_name_peek_node` | the root with its children up to the first `m_Name` or `name`, and that name, or `None`; cached process-wide by the root's `(m_Name, m_Type, m_Version)` (`:190-211`); see `ObjectReader.peek_name` | A | API | `test_rows_objects.py` peek_name; planned: peek suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.dump` | old-format writer; writes the root's fields for every node, so a tree of n nodes is n copies of the root's record (`:213-235`); see section 18, pre-v10 writing | C | L4 | `test_rows_objects.py` typetree_node_dumps; planned: divergence test | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.dump_blob` | blob writer; common strings by offset, except the one at offset 0 (`AABB`), written as a local string (`:237-278`); from version 19 each node record carries `m_RefTypeHash` (32 bytes a node, 24 before), so a node without it raises `struct.error` there and writes below 19 | A, C | L4 | `test_rows_objects.py` typetree_node_dumps; planned: save suite; divergence test | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.dump_structure` | one line per node, pre-order, joined by `\n`. A line is the indent, `m_Type`, a space, `m_Name`, then the literal text ` // ByteSize{`, the byte size in upper-case hex, `}, Index{`, `m_Index`, `},`, then 17 spaces (the space before a backslash continuation inside the f-string, and the next source line's 16-space indent), then `Version{`, `m_Version`, `}, TypeFlags{`, `m_TypeFlags`, `}, MetaFlag{`, `m_MetaFlag`, `}`. The braces are printed, and a field that is `None` prints as `None`: a node `(0, "int", "a", 4, 1)` gives `  int a // ByteSize{4}, Index{None},`, 17 spaces, `Version{1}, TypeFlags{None}, MetaFlag{None}`. The root takes `indent` (default two spaces) and each level adds two more (`:280-288`). AssetRipper's StructsDump is the stated model, not the format | A | API | `test_rows_objects.py` typetree_node_dumps; planned: binding | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `TypeTreeNode.to_dict`, `TypeTreeNode.to_dict_list` | the fields that are not `None`, `m_Children` included as the node objects; and one such dict per node in pre-order (`:290-299`) | A | API | `test_rows_objects.py` typetree_node_dumps; planned: binding | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| equality | `TypeTreeNode.__eq__`: fields and children (`:301-302`). It reads the other operand's `to_dict`, so another type raises `AttributeError`: "'int' object has no attribute 'to_dict'" for `5`, "'NoneType' object has no attribute 'to_dict'" for `None` (the cases tested), in `node == 5`, `node != 5`, `node == None`, `None == node` and `node in [None]`. With `__eq__` and no `__hash__`, the class is unhashable: `hash(node)` raises `TypeError` "unhashable type: 'TypeTreeNode'" | — | API | `test_rows_internals.py` special_methods_on_other_operands | pinned P0 (other operands, `hash`) |
| `TypeTreeNode.get_common_strings` | the TPK's common strings, all of them for `version=None` (the only call), offsets by cumulative length (`:308-328`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` type_indices_and_blob_trees_follow_python | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |

| `resources/lzma.tpk` | AssetRipper's TPK: LZMA, type tree information, 187,298 bytes compressed and 1,385,610 uncompressed in a 187,318-byte file, loaded at import into `Tpk.TPKTYPETREE` (`helpers/Tpk.py:20-39`, `:484`). unityrs loads it from a path (`DESIGN.md` section 4, `unityrs-tpk`) | A | L2b | `test_rows_objects.py` tpk_file_and_lookup; planned: TPK node-list diff | pinned P0; impl P3 |

## 11. Versions

Lines are in `helpers/UnityVersion.py`. `CANONICAL_DUMP.md` section 4.2 gives the packing rule.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UnityVersion` | an `int`, `major<<48 \| minor<<32 \| build<<16 \| type<<8 \| number`; compares with ints and with tuples, the tuple `as_tuple` gives (`UnityVersion.__eq__`, `UnityVersion.__ne__`, `UnityVersion.__lt__`, `UnityVersion.__le__`, `UnityVersion.__gt__`, `UnityVersion.__ge__`), and hashes as the int (`UnityVersion.__hash__`) (`:23`, `:112-143`). Against any other operand (`None`, a `float`, a `str`, `bytes` and a `list`, the cases tested) each of the six raises `NotImplementedError` "Unsupported comparison" (`:117`, `:127`, `:137`), and so do `None == v` and `v in [None]` | A | L1 | `test_unitypy_boost.py` version_packing; `test_rows_internals.py` special_methods_on_other_operands | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| `UnityVersion.from_str` | the DOTALL pattern (`:7-10`), one type character, `a b c f p x` else 255, no type packs `f`; spill-over of wide fields (`:63-88`). A string the pattern does not match raises `ValueError` "Invalid version string: " followed by the string (`:70-71`; `2017.4` and `abc`, the cases tested) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` version_strings_pack_as_unitypy_packs_them; `test_rows_internals.py` an_unmatched_version_string_raises | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |

| `UnityVersion.type` | `UnityVersionType` of the type byte; raises `ValueError` for bytes 6-254 (`:40-42`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` version_strings_pack_as_unitypy_packs_them | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |

## 12. UnityCN and the decryptor

Lines are in `helpers/ArchiveStorageManager.py`.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| encryption flag | `UsesAssetBundleEncryption` is `0x200` in `ArchiveFlagsOld` and `0x1400` in `ArchiveFlags` (`enums/BundleFile.py:12-26`); a set flag builds a decryptor (`files/BundleFile.py:123-124`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` bundle_layouts_names_and_child_failures | verified P0 (L1); impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageManager.set_assetbundle_decrypt_key` | a 16-byte key, `str` encoded with surrogateescape; else `ValueError` (`:16-22`) | A | API | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageDecryptor` | u32 unknown, data/key vectors and signature vectors (`:62-72`), 70 bytes right after the bundle's flags, before the alignment (`files/BundleFile.py:123-131`); no key (`ArchiveStorageManager.DECRYPT_KEY`) raises `LookupError` (`:74-85`); the AES-ECB signature must equal `ArchiveStorageManager.UNITY3D_SIGNATURE`, `#$unity3dchina!@`, else a plain `Exception` (`:12`, `:87-89`); index and substitute tables from the decrypted key's 32 nibbles: the first 16, and the next 16 read column by column (`:91-94`). Each LZ4 or LZMA block flagged `0x100` is decrypted with its block index; a stored block is read as it is; when the header's own flags hold `0x100`, the compressed blocks info is decrypted too, at index 0, before it is decompressed (`files/BundleFile.py:141`, `:512-513`) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageDecryptor.decrypt_block` | UnityPyBoost's `decrypt_block` when present (`:96-98`), else the Python loop (`:100-107`), which walks the block's LZ4 tokens (`:120-143`) and raises where the walk reads past the block (`UnityPyBoost.decrypt_block`): an LZMA data block whose flags hold `0x100` and whose walk reads one byte past it (a bundle built so, the case tested) loads with UnityPyBoost and, without it, raises `IndexError` "index out of bounds on dimension 1". A literal count that carries the walk past the block's end reads nothing there (`:133-135`), so a walk that ends past the block having read no byte past it raises nothing, and both loops return the same bytes (with `index` (7i+3) mod 16 and `substitute` 1 to 15 then 0, the blocks `10` and `30`, whose walks end at offsets 15 and 13, return `e7` and `c7`, the cases tested). Where the walk, the byte just past the block read as 0 as UnityPyBoost reads it, reads on past that byte, the Python loop raises the same way, and UnityPyBoost reads and writes memory past the bytes object it returns; no sample shows such a block (Tier U, `UnityPyBoost.decrypt_block`) | A; U for a walk that reads past the byte just past the block | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; `test_rows_internals.py` decrypt_block_past_the_block_end; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `UnityPyBoost.decrypt_block` | the C version of the loop: the same bytes as the Python loop where the walk reads no byte past the block, a literal count that carries it past the end reading nothing there (`:36-38`; the cases tested are the `ArchiveStorageDecryptor.decrypt_block` row's). It checks no bound (`UnityPyBoost/ArchiveStorageDecryptor.cpp:6-53`, `:76-82`), and reads the byte just past the block as 0, the NUL terminator of the bytes object it returns (`:68`), decrypting it in that terminator's place, outside the bytes returned (`:12`). So where the walk reads that byte and, given the value it decrypts to, no byte after it, UnityPyBoost returns with no error the bytes the Python loop holds when it raises `IndexError` "index out of bounds on dimension 1": the block, each byte the walk visits in it decrypted. The cases tested: with tables that leave each byte as it is, a match offset's second byte (`00 00`) and a length byte after a literal count of 15 (`f0`), each returned as it is; with `index` (7i+3) mod 16 and `substitute` 1 to 15 then 0, the blocks `80`, `81` and `82`, whose token decrypts to a literal count of 15 and whose length byte, read as 0, decrypts to 0x66, returned as `f7`, `fe` and `f5`. Where the walk reads on past that byte (with `index` `020d000a070c090f040b0a060f030406` and `substitute` `0a08040d0b08020a060707010a0b0104` the 0 decrypts to a length byte of 0xFF, so the walk of `80` reads on, the case tested), the Python loop raises the same way, and UnityPyBoost reads and writes memory past the bytes object (`:12`), so what it returns depends on that memory (source reading: no test runs UnityPyBoost there); no sample shows such a block (Tier U) | A; U for a walk that reads past the byte just past the block | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; `test_rows_internals.py` decrypt_block_past_the_block_end; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageDecryptor.decrypt`, `ArchiveStorageDecryptor.decrypt_byte` | the LZ4-token-aware byte decryption (`:109-143`) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption (the bytes of a pinned input, and a bundle whose blocks decrypt); planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageManager.decrypt_key` | AES-ECB-encrypt the key vector, XOR with data (`:33-37`), with pycryptodome's `Crypto.Cipher.AES`, imported there: without pycryptodome, `ArchiveStorageDecryptor` raises `ModuleNotFoundError` once a key is set, so no UnityCN bundle loads (section 1; the oracle pins pycryptodome) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageManager.read_vector` | 16 + 16 bytes and one skipped byte (`:25-30`) | A | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` unitycn_decryption; planned: UnityCN samples | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ArchiveStorageManager.brute_force_key` | tries every 16-word-character window of a file as the key (`:40-59`) | — | — | — | n/a |
| encrypted save | UnityPy never encrypts. `save_fs` XORs the whole `UsesAssetBundleEncryption` mask of the file's flag set out of both flags when any bit of it is set (`files/BundleFile.py:303-307`): with `ArchiveFlagsOld` (`0x200`) that clears it, but with `ArchiveFlags` (`0x1400`) a header holding only `0x1000`, or only `0x400`, is saved holding the other bit, and the saved bundle reads back as encrypted, raising `LookupError` without a key (section 19.2) | A; C (writing) for one bit of `ArchiveFlags`' mask (section 19.2) | L4 | `test_rows_containers.py` bundle_save_xors_the_whole_encryption_mask | pinned P0; impl P3 |

## 13. Streams

Lines are in `streams/EndianBinaryReader.py` and `streams/EndianBinaryWriter.py`. They are Python I/O plumbing; their observable rules sit in the rows that use them, and these are the ones that matter.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EndianBinaryReader` | picks a memoryview reader for bytes, a stream reader for a file object or a path, by input type; default endian `>` (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:33-69`). Which one an input gets decides the string rule below: `Environment` opens paths as streams (`environment.py:137`), the dumper loads bytes, and a bundle's entries are always memory. An object with `read` but no `seek` or `tell` is read into memory and gets the memory reader, whose `__init__` then gets the object itself and raises `TypeError` "memoryview: a bytes-like object is required, not 'ReadOnly'", `ReadOnly` being its class (`:54-59`, `:237-239`); a memory reader passed in raises the same way ("... not 'EndianBinaryReader_Memoryview'"), while a stream reader passed in is wrapped in a stream reader, which reads (`:52-53`). The loaders take a reader of either kind as it is (`helpers/ImportHelper.py:49-50`), and raise for the object with `read` alone (section 19.2) | A; B for an object with `read` but no `seek` or `tell`, and for a memory reader handed to `EndianBinaryReader` | L0, L1 | `test_rows_paths.py` a_path_reads_strings_with_the_stream_rule; `test_rows_loading.py` endian_binary_reader_inputs | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| `EndianBinaryReader_Memoryview`, `EndianBinaryReader_Streamable`, `EndianBinaryReader_Streamable_LocalFile` | the three readers (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:231-332`) | — | — | — | n/a |
| strings to NUL | surrogate-escaped UTF-8, at most 32767 bytes. A string whose NUL is in reach reads the same from both readers. With no NUL in reach, the memory reader returns 32767 bytes without consuming a NUL, or, when the window reaches the end, raises a plain `Exception` "String not terminated" (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:275-284`; `EndianBinaryReader.reNot0` is the search); the stream reader drops the last byte it read: 32767 bytes in for 32766 characters, or 9 characters from the last 10 bytes of a stream. It raises only from a `Position` past the end (after `align_stream` at 9 of 10 bytes, or set there), where its loop, which stops at `Position == Length` alone, reads nothing and raises `ValueError` "Unterminated string: [b'']"; at the end it returns `""` (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:136-144`) | A | L1 | `test_dump.py` l0_detection_and_name_rules (memory); `test_rows_paths.py` a_path_reads_strings_with_the_stream_rule (both) | verified P0 (L1); impl P1 |
| aligned strings | `EndianBinaryReader.read_aligned_string`: a length outside `0 < n ≤ remaining` reads `""` (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:266-273`). Only the pure-Python typetree reader (`byte_size == 0`) and `ShaderConverter`'s keywords (`export/ShaderConverter.py:596`, `:600`) use it. UnityPyBoost's typetree reader, the L2 path for every object with `byte_size > 0`, raises instead: a negative length is error kind `negative_length`, a length past the end `out_of_bounds` (`CANONICAL_DUMP.md` section 4.3) | A | L2 | `test_unitypy_boost.py` check_read_values (the `""` rule), error_kinds (UnityPyBoost's raises) | verified P0 (L2); impl P1 |
| reads past the end | `read` and `read_bytes` return the bytes that exist, with no error, on both readers; only the fixed-width primitives raise (`struct.error`). The memory reader moves `Position` by the size asked, past the end (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:249-256`), while the stream reader's is the stream's `tell()`, which stops at the end (`:290-300`): `read_bytes(5)` at 8 of 10 bytes leaves 13 on the memory reader and 10 on a stream reader of an `io.BytesIO`, of a file the builtin `open` gives and of one `LocalFileSystem.open` gives (the cases tested). So a bundle entry or a `.resS` range that runs past its data reads short (sections 5.2), and the texture, mesh or audio code works on what it got; a loop that stops at a `Position` past the data stops on the memory reader and reads on with the stream reader (section 5.2, `WebFile`) | A | L1, L5 | `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data; `test_rows_loading.py` the_stream_reader_stops_at_the_end | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| negative sizes and positions | the memory reader's `Position` is a plain attribute that takes any value, a negative one included, and its reads apply Python's indexing to it (`streams/EndianBinaryReader.py:231-262`, `:340-342`). A fixed-width primitive at `Position` P is `struct.unpack_from` at P, so a negative P counts from the end, and it raises `struct.error` when fewer bytes than its size lie from P to the end (`-2` for an `int`) or P is before the start. `read(n)`: `n` 0 reads `b""`; `n` -1 reads from P to the end, P counted from the end when negative, and leaves `Position` at the length; any other `n` reads the slice `view[P:P + n]`, Python's slice rules applying to a negative P or P + n, and moves `Position` by `n`: of 20 bytes, `read(-2)` at 5 reads `b""` and moves back to 3, `read(-5)` at 2 reads `view[2:-3]`, bytes 2 to 16, and `read(4)` at -2 reads `view[-2:2]`, `b""`. `read_byte_array` reads its length and then `read`s it, so a length of -1 reads the rest of the data (`:155-159`, `:249-256`). The stream reader refuses a negative `Position` (`ValueError` "Position cannot be negative", `:302-305`), and its `read` is the stream's own (`:295`), so a negative size follows that stream's rule: -1 reads to the end; below -1, a buffered file (the builtin `open`, or `fs.open`, which `Environment` uses for a path) raises `ValueError` "read length must be non-negative or -1", while an `io.BytesIO` reads to the end. Any signed value a file stores that UnityPy uses as a position or a size reaches these rules unchecked. The sites this inventory pins: a bundle directory entry's i64 offset and size, which the memory reader applies to the blocks' data whether the bundle came from bytes or a path (section 5.2, `File.read_files`); a WebFile entry's i32 offset and size, applied by the file's own reader, the stream reader for a plain file from a path and the memory reader otherwise, a brotli file's decompressed bytes from a path included (section 5.2, `WebFile`); a WebFile entry's i32 name length, which that reader reads the name with, so -1 reads the rest of the data as the name, and a length below -1 read by the memory reader moves the reader back into the header it has read, which the loop reads again, forever where it lands on an entry's start (section 5.2, `WebFile`, Tier U); a UnityWeb or UnityRaw bundle's i32 level count, past which `read_web_raw` skips `8 × (levelCount - 1)` bytes unchecked, so a count below 1 moves the reader back, before the file's start for -100: from a path `ValueError` "Position cannot be negative"; from bytes the fields after the level table are read at that position counted from the end, and `struct.error` "offset … out of range" comes where it lies below minus the file's length, as in a 105-byte bundle (section 5.1, `BundleFile.read_web_raw`); a blob typetree's node count and string-buffer size (section 9.3, `TypeTreeNode.parse_blob`); an object's data offset, an i64 from format 22 (section 8, `ObjectReader.from_reader`); and the 5.3-5.4 Shader blob's offsets and sizes and a Metal sub-program's header offset (section 15.5, `ShaderProgram` and `ShaderSubProgram`) | A | L1, L7 | `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data; `test_rows_exports.py` shader_text | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| `EndianBinaryWriter` | default endian `>`; `write_string_to_null` and `write_aligned_string` encode with surrogateescape (`streams/EndianBinaryWriter.py:9-130`) | A | L3 | `test_dump.py` resave_aligns_after_every_aligned_array_element; `write_string_to_null`'s surrogateescape: `test_rows_writing.py` bundle_save_packers (an entry name holding the byte 0xFF); `write_aligned_string`'s: `test_unitypy_boost.py` resave_shows_what_the_dict_lost | verified P0 (L3); impl P3 |

## 14. Classes

Every class in `classes/generated.py` is listed in Appendix A. They are attrs records built from the TPK by `TpkClassGenerator`; their behaviour is the typetree's (section 9). This section covers the classes UnityPy adds behaviour to, and the classes the export helpers read.

| `Object.set_object_reader` | sets `object_reader`; `read_typetree(wrap=True)` calls it (`classes/Object.py:17-18`) | A | API | `test_rows_typetrees.py` base_classes_and_records; planned: object-mode suite | pinned P0; impl P3 |

(`:87-88`); one with no `objects`, a raw reader (a registry hit on a `.resS` or on CAB `""` included), a BundleFile or a WebFile, raises `AttributeError` ("'EndianBinaryReader_Memoryview' object has no attribute 'objects'", `:90`); a missing path id raises `KeyError` (`classes/PPtr.py:43-90`) | A; B for an `int` or `None` key the loop reaches, before a match or with none, for a file detected as a ZIP that `find_file` loads from disk, and for a record other than a `.splitN` name that `find_file` finds by `path/name` from an `Environment.path` with a URL-style prefix (section 19.2) | L1 (env) | `test_rows_typetrees.py` negative_file_ids_index_from_the_end (the guards, and what a match holds); `test_rows_objects.py` monobehaviour_node_generator (a file id past the externals); `test_rows_environment.py` environment_registry_and_find_file (the external's path and `find_file`); `test_rows_files.py` environment_constructor_arguments (the keys that are not a `str`); planned: env dump | pinned P0; impl P1 |

| `Texture2D.image` | getter `Texture2DConverter.get_image_from_texture2d`; setter `set_image` (`classes/legacy_patch/Texture2D.py:8-11`, `:78`). The getter fetches the data before anything else (`export/Texture2DConverter.py:240-241`), so a 0×0 texture with no data raises as `Texture2D.get_image_data` does, where no CAB `""` is registered, instead of returning the empty image `parse_image_data` gives; where a zip's directory entry has registered CAB `""` (section 3), the empty path reads `b""` and a 0×0 texture returns that empty image (section 19.2) | A; B for a 0×0 texture where no CAB `""` is registered | L5 | `test_rows_textures.py` an_empty_texture_follows_an_empty_stream_path (a 0×0 texture); planned: L5 goldens per format (section 17) | pinned P0 (a 0×0 texture); impl P1 |

A texture with no pixel data keeps an empty stream path. ### 15.5 ShaderConverter

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |

| `ShaderConverter.export_shader` | 5.3-5.4 `m_SubProgramBlob` through LZ4, read big-endian (the reader's default) into a `ShaderProgram` before `m_Script` is touched, meant to fill each `GpuProgramIndex N` line of `m_Script`; 5.5 and up a non-empty `compressedBlob`, read little-endian (`:84`); else `m_Script` as UTF-8; every result starts with `ShaderConverter.HEADER`, the `NOTE: This is *not* a valid shader file` banner (`:30-55`). The plain path returns text for no version: before 5.5 `m_Script` is a string, and `bytes(m_Script)` raises `TypeError` "string argument without an encoding", on the blob path too; from 5.5 a Shader has no `m_Script`, so with an empty `compressedBlob` `bytes(None)` raises `TypeError` "cannot convert 'NoneType' object to bytes". On the blob path the raise depends on the blob's byte order: a blob that parses big-endian reaches the `TypeError`, and one with a sub-program whose fixed-width fields run past its end, or whose program type the enum lacks, raises `struct.error` or `ValueError` first (`ShaderProgram`, `ShaderSubProgram`). A little-endian one is read with a byte-swapped count and offsets: a count whose low byte is under 0x80 reads as a large positive one (256 as 65536), the sub-program table of that count is allocated first, a list of that many 8-byte slots, which raises `MemoryError` where the process cannot allocate it (a low byte of 0x7F asks for 2130706432 slots), and otherwise an entry read past the blob raises `struct.error`; a count whose low byte is 0x80 or more reads as negative (128 as -2147483648), the table is empty, no entry is read, and the blob path reaches the same `TypeError` as a blob that parses (`:546-559`; section 19.2) | A, C; U for a 5.3-5.4 blob that does not parse big-endian, or a `GpuProgramIndex` line its table cannot fill (section 19.2) | L7 | `test_rows_exports.py` shader_text | pinned P0; impl P3 |

| `ShaderProgram` | sub-program table of the blob's signed count, entry size 12 from 2019.3 and 8 before, a negative count giving an empty table; each entry's offset is a signed `int` that sets the memory reader's `Position` unchecked, so a negative offset counts from the blob's end and its sub-program is read by section 13's rules for a negative position: a code whose 4 bytes would run from -4 to index 0 reads `b""`, and a fixed-width field at -2 raises `struct.error` (`:543-568`). `Export` replaces each `GpuProgramIndex ` and the rest of its line (the regex `GpuProgramIndex (.+)`, whose `.` stops only at `\n`) with sub-program N's text, N being `int()` of that rest, in base 10, which allows whitespace around the number (ASCII space, `\t`, `\v`, `\f` and `\r`, a trailing `\r` included, so a CRLF line fills, and every non-ASCII character `str.isspace()` takes, such as a no-break space, `\x85` and `　`, but not `\x1c` to `\x1f`, which `str.isspace()` takes too), a sign, `_` between digits, and the decimal digits of any script, at most 4300 of them (`sys.get_int_max_str_digits()`'s default, the `_` not counted): 4301 digits raise `ValueError` "Exceeds the limit (4300 digits) for integer string conversion: value has 4301 digits; use sys.set_int_max_str_digits() to increase the limit". Anything else raises `ValueError` too (`0 }`, `0\rX`, `\x1c0`, a base prefix as in `0x0`, `0.0`, a `_` at either end). N indexes the table as a list: an N outside `[-len, len)` raises `IndexError`, and a negative one inside it counts from the end | A | L7 | `test_rows_exports.py` shader_text | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `ShaderSubProgram` | sub-program versions 201509030 to 202012090: the program type is read into `ShaderGpuProgramType` at once, so a type outside 0 to 31 raises `ValueError` while the sub-program is read (`:589`), and a fixed-width field past the data `struct.error`; four more bytes skipped from 201608170, keywords, local keywords only in `[201806140, 202012090)`, code (a negative keyword or local-keyword count reads none; the code is `read_byte_array`'s, read by section 13's rules, so a length of -1 reads the rest of the blob and another negative one the slice those rules give); code of length 0 prints `""` for every type; otherwise GL, GLES and console code as text and Metal as text after its entry name (behind the `0xF00DCAFE` header, or with four bytes dropped without it), each decoded as strict UTF-8, so code that is not UTF-8 raises `UnicodeDecodeError` (section 19.2). Metal code shorter than its four-byte tag, or than eight bytes with the `0xF00DCAFE` header, raises `struct.error`; the name starts at P, four without the header and the header's offset, unchecked, with it, and the memory reader looks for its NUL only in `code[max(P, 0):max(P + 32767, 0)]` (section 13): found, the text is what follows the NUL; not found, it raises a plain `Exception` "String not terminated" when that window reaches the code's end (P + 32767 at or past it: a P at or past the end included), and otherwise takes `code[P:P + 32767]` as the name and `code[P + 32767:]` as the text, Python slices. So a name of more than 32767 bytes leaves the rest as text (32768 bytes after the tag give the last byte), and a negative P counts from the code's start for the search and from its end for the text: -8 finds a NUL within 32759 bytes of the start, -32768 leaves the last byte (`:665-673`, section 19.2); DX9, DX10-level-9 and DX11 as a `// shader disassembly not supported on DXBC` comment, SPIR-V as nothing, any other type (Unknown, RayTracing) as a `//shader disassembly not supported on {type}` comment with the type's number (`:571-689`); an unknown program type raises `ValueError` (`:589`) | A, C | L7 | `test_rows_exports.py` shader_text | pinned P0; impl P3 |
