### 15.8 Image files by extension

`extractor.exportTexture2D` and `extractor.exportSprite` save the decoded image under the container's extension, and Pillow 9.5.0's `Image.save` picks its writer from that extension (section 15.7). The table below is what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py writers` prints in the oracle image, and `test_pillow_tables.py` writers_table_is_what_pillow_does requires this section to hold exactly that. Each row is a writer Pillow registers, with the extensions that select it, what it does with each mode the extractor can hand it, what the size and the file name change, and what unityrs writes:
- **The modes** are those of a decoded texture, `RGBA`, `RGB` and `L` (section 17), and `LA`, which `SpriteHelper.mask_sprite` gives a sprite of an `L` texture when it adds the mask with `putalpha` (section 15.2).
- **The size and file name** column comes from saving each mode the writer takes at 1×1, 15×15, 16383×1, 1×16383, 16384×1 and 1×16384, and under a second ASCII basename and a non-ASCII one, beside the 40×24 image named `image`. "The bytes hold the file's basename" means the two ASCII names give different bytes, so the acceptance compares with Pillow's save to the same path. It does so too where the bytes depend on how the extension is spelt (next item).
- **The extension** chooses the writer case-insensitively: Pillow lower-cases it before it looks the writer up, so `.PNG` is PNG. The writer may still read the file name itself, so the script saves every extension of every writer, in lower and in upper case, in each mode the writer takes, and the column states where the bytes differ with the basename masked. One writer's do: JPEG2000 writes a raw J2K codestream (`FF 4F FF 51`) only for a name ending `.j2k` in lower case, because `Jpeg2KImagePlugin._save` tests `filename.endswith(".j2k")` (`PIL/Jpeg2KImagePlugin.py:324` in Pillow 9.5.0), and a JP2 file (`00 00 00 0C 6A 50 20 20`) for `.J2K` and every other extension, `.j2c` and `.jpc` included. Every other writer writes the same bytes for every extension and spelling that chooses it, apart from the basename IM writes, extension included.
- **Where Pillow saves**, unityrs writes the same file (Tier A). The acceptance is byte equality with Pillow 9.5.0's `Image.save(path)` with no options, to the same path where the bytes hold the basename, in the oracle image, whose libraries the line under the table names. A PDF holds the time of the save in `/CreationDate` and `/ModDate`, so for PDF the two values are masked before comparing.
- **Where Pillow raises**, for the mode, the size or the file name, the extractor is Tier C (section 19.2): unityrs writes a PNG named `{fp}{extension}.png`. The same goes for an ICO with a side below 16: Pillow writes a file holding no image, because ICO keeps only the sizes of its default list (16×16 to 256×256) that fit the image. Neither PNG is written for an image with a zero side, which Pillow cannot write as a PNG either (`SystemError` "tile cannot extend outside image" at each size tested: 0×0, 0×3, 2×0 and 3×0): the 0×0 image of a texture of non-zero width and zero height (section 15.7), or a sprite's image whose `textureRect` rounds to a zero side (section 15.2). unityrs writes no file.

| format | extensions | `RGBA` | `RGB` | `L` | `LA` | size and file name | tier | unityrs writes, and the acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 16. Audio

`DESIGN.md` section 3 fixes the format list: Ogg, RIFF and m4a pass through; FSB5 PCM, Vorbis, ADPCM and FADPCM decode in pure Rust; MPEG and Opus are optional; anything else is reported unsupported. FMOD is never bundled (`DESIGN.md` section 2). Lines are in `export/AudioClipConverter.py`; fmod_toolkit is 0.1.3. **gap** in the tier column marks a deliberate `DESIGN.md` gap: UnityPy produces output through FMOD, and unityrs reports the format unsupported. Where UnityPy produces nothing (tracker and legacy audio), the row is Tier U instead: unityrs refuses the clip with a named `Unsupported` error where UnityPy returns `{}` silently, a documented divergence.

| format | UnityPy 1.25.2 | unityrs | tier | level | test | status |

| data source | `m_AudioData` when non-empty (old clips), else `m_Resource` when it is not `None`, through `ResourceReader`, else `ValueError` "AudioClip with neither m_AudioData nor m_Resource" (`:21-33`). A `StreamedResource` is truthy even when empty, so a clip with no data has its resource fetched: an empty `m_Source` is joined to `Environment.path` and raises `IsADirectoryError` when that is a directory and no CAB `""` is registered (section 5.2), while a CAB `""` (a zip's directory entry, section 3) answers it with `b""`, which reaches FMOD as a real resource of zero bytes does, and FMOD raises `FmodError`; the `ValueError` needs `m_Resource` to be `None` | same, except that a clip with `m_Size` 0 returns no samples (section 19.2) | A; B for an empty clip | L8 | `test_rows_export_helpers.py` an_empty_audio_clip_follows_an_empty_resource_path; planned: audio fixtures | pinned P0 (an empty clip); impl P3 |
| Ogg (`OggS`) | returned unchanged as `{name}.ogg` (`:35-37`) | passthrough | A | L8 | `test_rows_export_helpers.py` audio_dispatch; planned: audio fixtures | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| RIFF | returned unchanged as `{name}.wav` (`:38-39`) | passthrough | A | L8 | `test_rows_export_helpers.py` audio_dispatch; planned: audio fixtures | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| m4a (`ftyp` at offset 4) | returned unchanged as `{name}.m4a` (`:40-41`) | passthrough | A | L8 | `test_rows_export_helpers.py` audio_dispatch; planned: audio fixtures | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| anything else | `fmod_toolkit.raw_to_wav` with `m_Channels or 2` and `m_Frequency or 44100`; one WAV per subsound, `{name}.wav` or `{name}-{i}.wav` (`:43-49`) | FSB5 parser | A | L8 | `test_rows_export_helpers.py` audio_dispatch (the call); planned: audio fixtures | pinned P0 (the call); impl P3 |
| FSB5 PCM8, PCM16, PCM24, PCM32 | FMOD's samples as they are, behind fmod_toolkit's 44-byte RIFF header of the sample's width, one file a subsound, the second and later named `{name}-{i}.wav`. The bundled libfmod refuses a PCM8, PCM24 or PCM32 bank built here (`FmodError` VERSION), so only PCM16 reaches fmod_toolkit through FMOD. fmod_toolkit allocates the data's length plus 40 bytes and packs the data chunk's header at offset 36, so a subsound of fewer than 4 data bytes raises `struct.error` "pack_into requires a buffer of at least 44 bytes for packing 8 bytes at offset 36 (actual buffer size is N)", N being 40 plus the data's length; from 4 bytes the samples, put at offset 44, grow the buffer back (fmod_toolkit 0.1.3, `fmod.py` lines 142, 171-177 and 187). The raise ends the whole `raw_to_wav` call, so no subsound of the bank comes back. The cases tested: through FMOD, a mono PCM16 subsound of one sample (N 42), alone or beside one of four samples in either order, and a second subsound of no samples (N 40), last or before one of four samples, while one stereo sample, 4 bytes, writes 48 bytes; on a stand-in for FMOD's subsound, 0 to 3 bytes of PCM8, PCM16, PCM24 and PCM32. FMOD refuses to open a bank whose first subsound has no samples (`FmodError` PLUGIN, from `System.create_sound`, `fmod.py` line 61), so fmod_toolkit converts no subsound of it, whatever follows in the cases tested: mono PCM16, that subsound alone or before one subsound of no, one or four samples, or before two of four | pure Rust; where fmod_toolkit raises, each subsound as it writes one from 4 data bytes, and a bank whose first subsound has no samples refused (section 19.2) | Tol; C where fmod_toolkit raises on fewer than 4 data bytes; U for a bank whose first subsound has no samples | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs; `test_rows_audio.py` short_pcm_subsounds | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| FSB5 PCM float | FMOD's floats converted to int16 with numpy, `(v * 32768).clip(-32768, 32767)` truncated toward zero, in a 16-bit header; `convert_pcm_float=False` keeps them, WAV format 3 (fmod_toolkit 0.1.3, `fmod.py` lines 129-137, 195-198). The bundled libfmod refuses a PCM float bank built here (`FmodError` VERSION). The WAV data is half the float bytes when converted, so on a stand-in for FMOD's subsound one float raises the `struct.error` the FSB5 PCM row states (N 42) and two floats write 48 bytes; kept as floats, one float writes 48 bytes | pure Rust; where fmod_toolkit raises, the subsound as it writes one from 4 WAV data bytes (section 19.2) | Tol; C where fmod_toolkit raises on fewer than 4 WAV data bytes | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs; `test_rows_audio.py` short_pcm_subsounds | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| FSB5 Vorbis | FMOD decode to PCM | pure Rust | Tol | L8 | planned: against FMOD (needs a bank FMOD wrote: one built here, without Vorbis setup data, is refused with `FmodError` VERSION) | unverified (source reading); impl P3 |
| FSB5 IMA ADPCM | FMOD decode to PCM16 | pure Rust | Tol | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| FSB5 FADPCM | FMOD decode to PCM16 | pure Rust | Tol | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| FSB5 MPEG | FMOD decode to PCM16, 1152 samples a frame | optional feature | Tol | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| FSB5 Opus | FMOD decode to PCM | optional feature | Tol | L8 | planned: against FMOD (needs a bank FMOD wrote: one built here is refused with `FmodError` UNSUPPORTED) | unverified (source reading); impl P3 |
| any other codec (XMA, ATRAC9, VAG, HEVAG, GC ADPCM, …) | whatever the bundled `libfmod` decodes: banks built here of GC ADPCM, VAG, HEVAG, CELT and XWMA are refused with `FmodError` VERSION, XMA and ATRAC9 with UNSUPPORTED; a non-PCM result raises `TypeError`, not the `NotImplementedError` fmod_toolkit means to raise, because its message concatenates a `str` and a `SOUND_FORMAT` enum (fmod_toolkit 0.1.3, `fmod.py` line 139) | reported unsupported | gap | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| tracker and legacy audio (IT, MOD, S3M, XM, AIFF, pre-5 MP3) | FMOD opens the file as one sound with no subsounds, and fmod_toolkit exports subsounds only, so `samples` is `{}` and the extractor writes nothing: an AIFF, a MOD and an MP3 built here; IT, S3M and XM were not built | refused with a named `Unsupported` error, where UnityPy writes nothing and raises nothing: a documented divergence | U | L8 | `test_rows_exports.py` audio_codecs | pinned P0 (AIFF, MOD, MP3); impl P3 |

## 17. TextureFormat matrix

One row per `TextureFormat` value (78; 37-40 are unassigned). Lines are in `export/Texture2DConverter.py` unless named; the conversion table is `Texture2DConverter.CONV_TABLE` (`:487-566`).

**Columns.**
- **decoder**: the format's `CONV_TABLE` entry. Pillow raw `M` into `X` is `Image.frombytes(X, size, data, "raw", M)`, and Pillow `bcn` N into `X` the same with Pillow 9.5.0's BCn decoder `N` (`BcnDecode.c`); ", bands (…)" is the channel reorder that follows (`:331-344`). `half`, then … converts half floats to bytes first (`:435-447`). `rg` (…) names the arguments `CONV_TABLE` passes to `rg` (`:459-468`). texture2ddecoder (1.0.5) returns BGRA, which Pillow's `BGRA` raw mode turns into RGBA. "astcenc LDR, W×H blocks" is astc-encoder-py 0.1.12 with the LDR profile and `USE_DECODE_UNORM8` (`:369-387`). "crunch, then …" unpacks first (gates). "none" is a format with no entry.
- **conversion**: how the bytes become pixels, as shown by the pixel cases of the matrix test (below).
- **mode**: the Pillow mode UnityPy returns. Every image is then flipped top to bottom (`:317-318`).
- **gates**: what `parse_image_data` does before decoding, observed on a 1×1 texture with stand-ins for the decoders, the deswizzle and the crunch unpackers. `pad W×H` is the block padding before decoding and the crop after (`Texture2DConverter.TEXTURE_FORMAT_BLOCK_SIZE_TABLE`, `:22-50`, `:314-315`); `no pad` means none. `sw W×H` is the Switch texel block (`TextureSwizzler.TEXTURE_FORMAT_BLOCK_SIZE_MAP`, `helpers/TextureSwizzler.py:73-103`), used when `TextureSwizzler.is_switch_swizzled` holds, in place of the block padding (`:279-291`); ", read as `F`" means the swizzled data is decoded as format `F`; `sw —` means a swizzled texture of that format raises `KeyError`, which makes that case Tier U whatever the row's result (section 19.2). `xbox` swaps byte pairs on XBOX360, before anything else, crunch unpacking included (`:274-275`, `:569`). "crunch:" names the unpacker each version gets: 2017.3 is the boundary (`:297-306`).
- **UnityPy 1.25.2**: the result of `parse_image_data` on an 8×8 texture, StandaloneWindows64, version 2021.3.1f3, on `0x30` bytes, as many as a real 8×8 texture of the format holds; the first line cited is the format's `CONV_TABLE` entry, when it has one. `raises` names the exception, module-qualified when it is not a builtin, and its second line is where it left `Texture2DConverter.py`. `works`, `wrong` and `unverified` return an image in the listed mode, checked down to the pixels on input built from the format's layout (raw formats from a byte pattern, block formats from one solid-colour block per block position): `works` gives the pixels that layout fixes and the conversion column states; `wrong` gives the wrong pixels the conversion column states, not those (section 19); `unverified` has no reference to check against, and the pixels UnityPy returns are pinned. `crunch` rows are decoded with the unpacker handing the data on unchanged, as their base format's data: their conversion is checked, and crunch decoding is not.
- **`set_image` writes**: the format `image_to_texture2d` writes for that target from an RGBA image (`:118-234`); "(`F` from an RGB image)" when an RGB image gets another, and "(an RGB image with no zero side raises …)" when such an RGB image raises (section 18.1, which states what an image with a zero side does); "holding plain ETC1" when the data is what it writes for `ETC_RGB4`; "Tier C (writing)" when it raises, or when its own reader cannot read what it wrote.
- **tier** and **status**: section 0. The tier follows the result: `works` and `crunch` A, `raises` B where section 17.1 names a reference and U where it cannot (`ARGBFloat`), `wrong` C, `unverified` U. It is the tier on every platform but one: a Switch-swizzled texture of a `sw —` format is U (section 19.2). Tier A rows get L5 goldens against the oracle, Tier B rows the reference section 17.1 names, Tier C rows a pinned divergence test; `DESIGN.md` section 5 puts BC7, DXT1, DXT5, RGBA32 and RGB24 in P1 and every other format in P3.

`test_texture_matrix.py` texture_matrix_matches_the_oracle builds every cell of every row from the oracle and the pixel cases, and requires the matrix to hold exactly that, so a change to any cell fails it. No builder reads this file: `test_texture_matrix.py` no_cell_is_built_from_this_file builds every row again in a new process, from a copy of this file with every matrix cell but the format names changed, and requires the same rows. pixels_match_the_matrix checks the pixels of every row that returns an image; switch_and_xbox_data_decode_as_the_gates_say decodes each pixel case swizzled with the stated Switch block, and byte-swapped on XBOX360; half_values_outside_a_byte_raise shows the half-float raises the `RHalf` and `RGBAHalf` rows state.

The Tier B count is 21, as `DESIGN.md` section 3 says: `BC6H`; the half and float formats `RHalf`, `RGHalf`, `RFloat`, `RGFloat`, `RGBAFloat`, `RGBFloat`; the eight `_SIGNED` raw formats; `RG16`, `RG32`, `R16`, `RGBA64`, `RGB9e5Float` and `YUY2`. `RGBAHalf` and `RGB48` return an image, so they are not Tier B. `ARGBFloat` raises too, but no reference can be named for it, so it is Tier U (section 17.1).

Two more behaviours of `parse_image_data` apply to every format: a zero width or height returns an empty RGBA image, and empty data raises `ValueError` (`:265-269`); a format number outside the enum raises `ValueError` (`:271-272`). Through `Texture2D.image`, a texture with no data reaches the first only where CAB `""` is registered (section 3, `Environment.load_zip_file`): its fetch then gives `b""`, so a 0×0 texture returns the empty image and one of non-zero size raises `ValueError` "Texture2D has no image data"; where none is, it raises while its data is fetched (section 14.2).

### 17.1 Tier B references

Each format UnityPy raises on has the reference below, independent of UnityPy. unityrs returns exactly what the reference gives for the stored bytes: the mode stated, every pixel equal, flipped top to bottom as every format is (section 17). `tools/parity/references.py` implements each reference, and `references.decode(name, data, width, height)` is what the divergence test compares unityrs with. `test_texture_matrix.py` tier_b_references_hold_their_stated_values checks it on the values this section states.

**Short data.** Data shorter than the texture needs, its pixels or, for `BC6H`, its whole 4×4 blocks (`references.needed_bytes`), is an error for every format: `references.decode` raises `ValueError`, as Pillow's decoders raise "not enough image data", and unityrs reports the texture as truncated instead of decoding pixels for bytes that do not exist. A `.resS` read past the end returns such data (section 5.2). Bytes past what the texture needs are ignored, as Pillow ignores them. `test_texture_matrix.py` short_data_raises_for_every_reference checks both, one byte short and with no data, for every format.

The closed forms, which `BC6H`'s reference (the last row) also ends with:
- **the half and float rule**: each channel, IEEE 754 binary16 or binary32, little-endian, becomes `floor(min(max(v, 0), 1) * 255 + 0.5)`, computed exactly in f64, and NaN becomes 0. So 0.5 gives 128 (the only tie in [0, 1]), 1.0 and above 255, and anything below 0 gives 0. The Tier C rows `RGBAHalf` and `ASTC_HDR_*` use the same rule (section 19.1).
- **the unsigned rule**: each n-bit little-endian channel becomes its high byte, `v >> (n - 8)`, as Pillow 9.5.0's 16-bit raw modes reduce it (the `RGB48` rule, section 19.2). `0xFF00` gives 255 and `0x00FF` gives 0.
- **the signed rule**: each n-bit two's-complement channel is offset by 2^(n-1) and then reduced to its high byte, so the most negative value gives 0, 0 gives 128 and the largest gives 255, for 8 and 16 bits alike.
- **channels**: a one-channel format returns RGB `(v, 0, 0)`, as `R8` does, and a two-channel format `(r, g, 0)`, as `BC5` does.

Y 16 gives black and Y 235 white. An odd width is Tier U: no sample shows how Unity stores the last pixel | planned: divergence test against `references.decode` | planned (P3) |

| `BC6H` | 16-byte blocks of 4×4 | RGB | the unsigned form, the only one `GRAPHICS_TO_TEXTURE_MAP` sends to `BC6H` (`RGB_BC6H_UFloat`; `enums/GraphicsFormat.py`), decoded as the Khronos Data Format Specification 1.3, section 20.2, decodes it (the rules of `ARB_texture_compression_bptc`): its mode, endpoint layout, partition, anchor and weight tables (Tables 114, 118, 119, 121 and 122), the unsigned unquantization, Equation 2's interpolation `((64 - w) * e0 + w * e1 + 32) >> 6` and the final `(i * 31) >> 6`, giving a binary16 value per channel, which the half and float rule takes to 8 bits; a reserved mode gives (0, 0, 0). `references.bc6h_block` writes it out. Half `0x307F` (0.1405) gives 36, `0x3B27` (0.894) 228 and `0x3C9B` (1.15) 255; a 10-bit red endpoint of 512 interpolated at weight 34 towards 0 gives half `0x1D17` and red 1 (`test_texture_matrix.py` bc6h_reference_is_the_specification). RGB, because the format holds no alpha (the section returns 1.0) and Pillow's BC6H decoder, the one UnityPy calls in mode `RGBA`, decodes only into `RGB`. The data is decoded at the size padded to whole 4×4 blocks and cropped, as UnityPy pads every `BC` format; data short of those blocks raises (short data, above). Neither BC6H decoder in the oracle image is the reference, because each departs from section 20.2 (section 19.2) | planned: divergence test against `references.decode` | planned (P3) |

`ARGBFloat` is Tier U. Two sources disagree on its layout: its name says A, R, G, B, while UnityPy's `CONV_TABLE` reorders it with bands (2, 1, 0, 3), as if stored B, G, R, A. With no sample and no specification of the layout to decide between them, no reference can be named, and unityrs refuses it with a named `Unsupported` error. UnityPy raises too.

## 18. Writing

`DESIGN.md` section 3 sets the rules: object re-save byte-equal; file and bundle save semantically equal and idempotent, with an optional UnityPy-layout mode byte-equal to `save(packer='none')`, except that a save of a format-9 bundle follows HEAD wherever HEAD writes it (section 20); compressed packers equal after decompression, byte-equal LZ4HC only with liblz4 HC level 9; `File.get_writeable_cab` with UnityPy's naming rule; `set_image` equal after re-decode. Where UnityPy fails to write, the item is Tier C (writing), and its row here or in section 19 states the acceptance rule: pre-v10 and v11 typetree writing, an empty-class ReferencedObject, `set_image` to BC7 and to ETC_RGB4Crunched, `WebFile.save` of a writer entry, `set_image` from an image with no zero side in a mode Pillow cannot pack (section 18.1), `Environment.save` of a file keyed by a stream `name` that is not a `str`, `SerializedFile.get_writeable_cab` from a bundle or WebFile (which raises, so its GUID rule is the reference, not UnityPy's output), `BundleFile.save` of a UnityWeb or UnityRaw bundle of a format above 3 but 6 (`save_web_raw`), and `BundleFile.save` with `original` and no blocks or with encryption flags, at every format: HEAD fails the same way, so a format-9 bundle takes these rules too (section 20). Where neither a sample nor an independent reference says what to write, or what UnityPy writes is not characterised, the item is Tier U and unityrs refuses it: `set_image` with mips on a Switch-swizzled texture and `set_image` on XBOX360 of an image with no zero side to a format the reader byte-swaps (section 19.2), and `set_image` of a zero-size image on a Switch-swizzled texture, which is not characterised (section 19.1).

### 18.1 Image modes `set_image` packs

`image_to_texture2d` flips the image and packs it with `Image.tobytes("raw", M)`, where `M` is the raw mode the target needs, without converting the image first (`export/Texture2DConverter.py:118-234`, `:223`, `:228`). Pillow 9.5.0 packs only some image modes into each `M`; for any other it raises `ValueError` "No packer found from X to M", for an image with no zero side. For an image with a zero side, `tobytes` returns `b""` before it looks a packer up, whatever the mode (Pillow 9.5.0, `Image.py` lines 756-760). `Texture2D.set_image` opens a path or a stream with `Image.open` and passes the image on in the mode it opened in (`classes/legacy_patch/Texture2D.py:25-26`). So an indexed PNG (mode `P`) or a grey one (`L`) with no zero side raises for every target, and such an `RGB` image raises for `Alpha8`. The table is what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py packers` prints in the oracle image, for every mode Pillow 9.5.0's `Image.new` accepts, and `test_pillow_tables.py` packers_table_is_what_pillow_does requires this section to hold exactly that:

| raw mode | targets that pack it | image modes Pillow 9.5.0 packs into it |
|---|---|---|
| `A` | `Alpha8` | `RGBA`, `LAB` |
| `R` | `EAC_R`, `EAC_R_SIGNED`, `R16`, `R8`, `RFloat`, `RHalf` | `RGB`, `RGBX`, `RGBA` |
| `RGB` | `ATC_RGB4`, `BGR24`, `PVRTC_RGB2`, `PVRTC_RGB4`, `RGB24`, `RGB565`, `RGB9e5Float`; on a Switch-swizzled texture they pack `RGBA` | `RGB`, `RGBX`, `RGBA` |
| `RGBA` | every other target (64) | `RGB`, `RGBA` |

Packed into none of them: `1`, `L`, `I`, `F`, `P`, `CMYK`, `YCbCr`, `HSV`, `RGBa`, `BGR;15`, `BGR;16`, `BGR;24`, `LA`, `La`, `PA`, `I;16`, `I;16L`, `I;16B`, `I;16N`.
`Image.convert("RGBA")` raises for: `BGR;15`, `BGR;16`, `BGR;24`, `La`.

- **Where Pillow packs the mode**, unityrs writes what UnityPy writes (Tier A). This includes `LAB` into `Alpha8`: Pillow packs LAB's `A` band, the a* channel, as the alpha.
- **Where it does not**, and the image has no zero side, UnityPy raises on a valid request, a failure to write, so the case is Tier C (writing) (`DESIGN.md` section 3, Writing; section 19.2). unityrs converts the image with Pillow 9.5.0's `Image.convert("RGBA")` and writes that. The reference is UnityPy's `set_image` of the converted image, which packs, because every target packs `RGBA`.
- **A mode `Image.convert("RGBA")` raises for** is Tier U for an image with no zero side: unityrs refuses it with a named `Unsupported` error. UnityPy raises too.
- **An image with a zero side** reaches no packer, whatever its mode. Run as section 19.1's zero-size row runs an `RGBA` image (on a texture that is not Switch-swizzled, in the configurations that row names; at 0×0, 0×4, 4×0, 0×5 and 5×0; to every target), `image_to_texture2d` does with an image of every mode `Image.new` accepts what it does with an `RGBA` one: it hands etcpak the same arguments, raises the same error, or returns the same bytes under the same format, but that an `RGB` image is written as `ASTC_RGB_` for an `ASTC_HDR_` target, as a non-empty one is (section 17). So `Texture2D.set_image` of an `L`, `P`, `La` or `BGR;15` image of 0×0, 0×4 or 4×0 to `RGBA32` writes 0 bytes, with the image's size, under `RGBA32`, where the same mode at 4×4 raises "No packer found from X to RGBA". Section 19.1's zero-size row decides the tier for every mode, as for an `RGBA` image.

### 19.2 Decided while writing this inventory

Defects found while writing this file, each decided by `DESIGN.md` section 3's definitions, which lists them too. UnityPy's side of a test named here is pinned now where the test exists; the divergence test proper compares unityrs with it once unityrs implements the row.

| RGB48 decodes wrong | Pillow's `RGB;16` is packed 5-6-5, two bytes per pixel, so a pure red `(0xFFFF, 0, 0)` decodes as white (`export/Texture2DConverter.py:556`) | C | each 16-bit channel reduced to its high byte, `v >> 8`, as Pillow 9.5.0's raw mode `RGB;16L` reduces it (rounding, `round(v * 255 / 65535)`, would give 254 for `0xFF00` and 1 for `0x00FF`); the reference is the stored values reduced so | L5 | `test_texture_matrix.py` pixels_match_the_matrix (UnityPy's side); `test_texture_matrix.py` rgb48_reference_is_the_high_byte | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| `set_image` to BC7 raises | `etcpak.compress_bc7(data, w, h, None)`; etcpak 0.9.14 rejects `None` parameters with `ValueError` (`export/Texture2DConverter.py:95`) | C (writing) | writes what etcpak 0.9.14's `compress_bc7` writes with a default `BC7CompressBlockParams()`, the call UnityPy means to make, on the data `image_to_texture2d` hands it: the image flipped top to bottom, padded to whole 4×4 blocks by `pad_image` and packed as RGBA (`export/Texture2DConverter.py:129-130`, `:222-223`). On a texture where `TextureSwizzler.is_switch_swizzled` holds, the image is padded instead to `TextureSwizzler.get_padded_texture_size` with BC7's 4×4 block and the blob's GOBs per block, compressed the same way, and the compressed bytes swizzled with `TextureSwizzler.swizzle` (`:205-218`, `:220-232`); this is a single level, since mips on such a texture are Tier U. Acceptance: byte equality with those bytes, which makes the writer Tier A against that encoder. | a sprite triangle with a collinear destination | `copy_triangle` takes the affine path whenever the destination's edge offsets differ from the source's, and solves for the transform from the destination's three integer corners; three collinear corners, a zero-area triangle that rounding positions to integers can make of a real one, make the matrix singular, and `numpy.linalg.LinAlgError` escapes `Sprite.image` and the extractor (`export/SpriteHelper.py:243`, `:270-295`) | C | that triangle copies nothing, and the sprite's other triangles are copied as UnityPy copies them. No reference decides this, so it is a chosen convention: Pillow's polygon mask of a zero-area triangle covers the pixels on its line, and on the translation path UnityPy copies those (`SpriteHelper.copy_triangle`'s row, Tier A), so the same destination gets pixels from a source with its offsets and none from a mirrored one; but no affine transform maps a source triangle onto a zero-area destination, so no source pixel is defined for them | L5 | `test_rows_extractor.py` sprite_mesh_rendering (UnityPy's side); planned: divergence test | pinned P0; impl P1 |
| a sprite's alpha texture that `Image.merge` refuses | `SpriteHelper.get_image` merges the first three bands of the texture's decoded image with the first band of the alpha texture's (`export/SpriteHelper.py:50-52`), a texture with a zero side decoding to a 0×0 RGBA image whatever its format: a `BC4` texture with no zero side decodes to `L`, one band, so the merge raises `ValueError` "wrong number of bands", and otherwise decoded sizes that differ raise "size mismatch", a zero side beside a texture with none included (section 15.2) | U | refuses such a sprite's image with a named `Unsupported` error, where UnityPy raises too: no sample shows such a sprite, or how Unity combines its two textures | L5 | `test_rows_images.py` sprite_alpha_merge_needs_like_sizes_and_three_bands (UnityPy's side) | pinned P0; impl P1 |

## 20. HEAD tier

The HEAD oracle is UnityPy git `417d595`. Lines below are in that tree. The HEAD tier has two parts (`DESIGN.md` section 3). The writing part is pinned now: unityrs is to write as HEAD's writer does on a bundle save with `lz4hc` and on every save of a format-9 bundle HEAD can write, and `test_rows_writing.py` head_saves runs HEAD's own writer against the rules below. The reading part, unityrs's dicts equal to HEAD's on SerializedFile 23-26 and bundle format 9, is a target, not a measurement: it is to be checked on Unity 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures, which P0 is to build and which do not exist yet, and its rows below pin only the 1.25.2 side and HEAD's reader on HEAD's own saves. No comparison with HEAD's dicts has been run. The image installs 1.25.2 only, so the writing rows run HEAD's own `helpers/CompressionHelper.py` and `files/BundleFile.py`, kept byte for byte in `tools/parity/tests/head_417d595/` and loaded inside 1.25.2 by `tools/parity/tests/head_oracle.py`, which checks each file's git blob id first.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | HEAD `417d595` | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SerializedFile v23 | no gate: a file of format 23 laid out as 22 parses as 22 (a real v23 file's types are not read as HEAD reads them) | per-type `TypeTreeNodeInfoHandler` in place of the blob (`files/SerializedFile.py:141-144`, `helpers/TypeTreeNode.py:342-394`) | L1, L2 | `test_rows_files.py` formats_past_22_read_as_22 (the 1.25.2 side); HEAD: planned 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures, since the image has no HEAD oracle | pinned P0 (the 1.25.2 side); impl P3 |
| SerializedFile v24, v25 | no gate: read as 22 | no gates of their own | L1, L2 | `test_rows_files.py` formats_past_22_read_as_22 (the 1.25.2 side); HEAD: planned 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures, since the image has no HEAD oracle | pinned P0 (the 1.25.2 side); impl P3 |
| SerializedFile v26 | no gate: read as 22, with no shared subtrees | shared subtrees after the ref types, resolved by hash (`files/SerializedFile.py:331-338`) | L1, L2 | `test_rows_files.py` formats_past_22_read_as_22 (the 1.25.2 side); HEAD: planned 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures, since the image has no HEAD oracle | pinned P0 (the 1.25.2 side); impl P3 |
| bundle format 9 | no gate: read with format 8's layout, with no block offsets | a u64 offset per block, used to seek each block (`helpers/CompressionHelper.py:158-172`, `files/BundleFile.py:485-491`) | L1 | `test_rows_files.py` formats_past_22_read_as_22 (the 1.25.2 side); `test_rows_writing.py` head_saves (HEAD's reader on HEAD's own format-9 saves); HEAD on real bundles: planned 6000.4/6000.5 fixtures | pinned P0 (the 1.25.2 side, HEAD's reader on its own saves); impl P3 |
| `lz4hc` packer | absent: `BundleFile.save` raises `NotImplementedError`. The tuple packer `(195, 3)` writes header flags `0xC3` (the blocks info LZ4HC, at the end, with the directory) and 1.25.2's blocks: the concatenated entries in 128 KiB chunks, each compressed by liblz4 HC level 9 and flagged 3, except a chunk whose compressed form is larger than the chunk, which is stored and flagged 0 (`chunk_based_compress`, section 6). So its blocks differ from HEAD's wherever a chunk that grows comes while the compressed output before it is under 0x20000 bytes (1.25.2 stores it, HEAD compresses it), or a chunk that does not grow comes after that much (1.25.2 compresses it, HEAD stores it); a chunk that compresses to exactly its own size does not grow, so 1.25.2 keeps it compressed | `save_fs(195, 3)` (`files/BundleFile.py:228-229`), through HEAD's own `chunk_based_compress` (`helpers/CompressionHelper.py:182-236`, called with the bundle's format at `files/BundleFile.py:310`): the concatenated entries in 128 KiB chunks, each compressed by liblz4 HC level 9 (`:97-105`); a chunk is kept compressed and flagged 3 while the compressed output written before it is under 0x20000 bytes, even when compressing made it larger, and from the first chunk that starts at or past 0x20000 every chunk is stored and flagged 0 (`:216-218`). From format 9 the blocks info gives each block's offset and each block is padded to 16 bytes (`:225`, `:230-234`). HEAD's `lz4` packer `(194, 2)` takes the same path at the same level. unityrs accepts `lz4hc` and writes that, byte-equal with HEAD's save (`DESIGN.md` section 3, Writing); 1.25.2's tuple packer is no reference for it | L4 | `test_rows_writing.py` bundle_save_packers (the 1.25.2 side: `lz4hc`, and the tuple's blocks on several chunks); `test_rows_containers.py` compression_helpers (a chunk that compresses to its own size); `test_rows_writing.py` head_saves (HEAD's side: the rule at formats 8 and 9 on several chunks, compressible, random and mixed, and the save that calls it) | pinned P0; impl P3 |
| save of a format-9 bundle | reads the bundle with format 8's layout and saves it so under version 9, with every packer: 10-byte block entries, no offset, no padding (section 18, `BundleFile.save_fs`), and its own chunk rule, under which an LZMA block that grows is stored and flagged 0 (section 6, `chunk_based_compress`). HEAD's reader takes each entry for 18 bytes. In a save of one block it reads the block's offset from the directory's count and the first directory entry's offset, which seeks the block past the data, and the directory's count from that entry's offset, 0: a block 1.25.2 stored (a `none` save, or an `lz4` or `lzma` one whose one chunk grew under compression) it reads as empty, a bundle with no entries, and a compressed one it fails to decompress (`LZ4BlockError` for `lz4`, `struct.error` for `lzma`). A save of more blocks (`lz4` on a payload of more than one chunk) it reads with an outcome that depends on the bytes it lands on: of the inputs `head_saves` builds, it fails while it reads the directory (`Exception` "String not terminated" for two blocks and `struct.error` for three), fails to decompress (`LZ4BlockError`, for two compressed blocks of zeros behind an empty first entry), or loads a bundle with no entries (two stored blocks of random data behind an empty first entry). A save with no block (`lz4` or `lzma` on an empty payload) it reads whole, with its entries. 1.25.2 reads each of HEAD's 18-byte entries as 10 bytes, so it reads the entries after the first, and the directory after them, from the wrong bytes: a save of HEAD's with one block (`none`; `lzma` on a payload that is not empty; another packer on a payload of one chunk) it reads as a bundle with no entries, the directory's count being the high half of that block's offset 0. In a save of two blocks (`lz4` or `lz4hc` on a payload of one to two chunks) it takes the high half of the second block's uncompressed size for the flags of a second block of size 0, and that size's low half for the directory count's high half: one whose second block holds 0x8000 to 0xFFFF bytes before compression, flags 0 and a negative count, it reads as a bundle with no entries, and any other it fails to load, with `struct.error` (reading a count above 0's entries, or decompressing the empty LZMA block that flags 1 name, from 0x10000 bytes) or, for a second block of 0x20000 bytes, `LZ4BlockError`. A save of three or more blocks, whose second block holds 0x20000 bytes, it fails to load, with an error that depends on the bytes it lands on (`LZ4BlockError` for three blocks and `struct.error` for four, of the inputs `head_saves` builds); a save with no block (an empty payload under a compressing packer) has nothing to misread, and it reads the entries | every packer through HEAD's `chunk_based_compress` with the bundle's format (`files/BundleFile.py:310`): each block entry carries a u64 offset (`helpers/CompressionHelper.py:174-179`); `none` writes one block at offset 0, unpadded, of size 0 for an empty payload (`:196-197`); `lz4`, `lz4hc` and a tuple follow the `lz4hc` row's chunk rule; `lzma` writes the payload in chunks of 0xFFFFFFFF bytes (`:258-263`), so one block for a payload under 4 GiB, compressed and flagged 1 even when it grew; a compressing packer writes no block at all for an empty payload (`range(0, 0, chunk)`, `:211`); each block's offset is the bytes written before it, and every block of a compressing packer, the last included, is padded to 16 bytes (`:225`, `:230-234`); the reader seeks each block by its offset (`files/BundleFile.py:485-491`). HEAD fails to write where 1.25.2 does (`files/BundleFile.py:168-169`, `:305-308`, the same code as 1.25.2's): `original` on a bundle with no blocks raises `AttributeError`, since `_block_info_flags` is set only by a bundle that had one, and a header whose flags hold a bit of the encryption mask is saved with the whole mask XORed out, which HEAD's reader reads back as encrypted (`LookupError` without a key). unityrs writes HEAD's save, byte-equal, except in those two cases, which follow the Tier C (writing) rules of section 19.2 (`BundleFile.save` with `original` and no blocks, `BundleFile.save_fs` encryption flags), HEAD's reader being the one that reloads the saved bundle (`DESIGN.md` section 3, Writing); 1.25.2's save of such a bundle is no reference | L4 | `test_rows_writing.py` head_saves (HEAD's save with `none`, `lz4`, `lz4hc`, `lzma`, `original` and a tuple at formats 8 and 9, read back by HEAD's reader, an empty payload included; HEAD's two failures; 1.25.2's format-9 save, and the two readers on each other's, by number of blocks, none included, by stored or compressed block, by the size of a second block, and by the bytes of two blocks) | pinned P0; impl P3 |

## 21. Keeping this file complete

`tools/parity/inventory.py` lists UnityPy's public surface and checks that this file names all of it:

```sh
scripts/dev python tools/parity/inventory.py list [--json]
scripts/dev python tools/parity/inventory.py check
```

| group | what | named here as |
|---|---|---|
| `modules` | every `.py` file under `UnityPy/` (78) | its path in a code span, alone or with `:line`; a path names its module and nothing else |
| `classes` | every public class a module defines (955, the generated classes included) | its name in a code span |
| `functions` | every public module-level function (126) | `Module.function` in a code span |
| `data` | every public name a module binds at its top level whose value is not a class, function, module or typing construct: tables, registries, settings, caches. The top level includes the blocks of its `if`, `for`, `while`, `try`, `with` and `match` statements, and a `for` loop's target is bound too (`Texture2DConverter.tf`); a name bound only where the import does not run (`if __name__ == "__main__":`) is not the module's (73) | `Module.NAME` in a code span |
| `methods` | every public callable or property a class defines in its own body, for every class UnityPy defines; for the generated classes, the members a `classes/legacy_patch` module adds. A special method (`PPtr.__bool__`) counts where a UnityPy module's own code defines it: not one attrs, `dataclasses`, `namedtuple` or `enum` generates, nor the constructor's `__init__` and `__new__`, which calling the class runs (267) | `Class.member` in a code span, or a compact row (below) |
| `fields` | every public attrs field a class declares itself, for every attrs class UnityPy defines, the 869 generated classes included (8,456) | `Class.field` in a code span, or a compact row: Appendix A for the generated classes, Appendix B for the rest |
| `attributes` | every other public attribute of a class UnityPy defines outside `classes/generated.py`, enums aside: a name its body assigns or annotates (a class constant, a dataclass field, a declared instance attribute), a name its own methods assign through their first parameter (`self.files = {}`), and a namedtuple's fields; a name a base class has is named under the base (145) | `Class.attribute` in a code span, or a compact row: Appendix B |
| `exports` | every public name a package's `__init__.py` imports from another UnityPy module or assigns as an alias (924) | the dotted path from `UnityPy` (`UnityPy.files.File`), or a compact row: section 2.1 |
| `aliases` | every public name a module other than a package's `__init__.py` binds at its top level to a class or function that another UnityPy module, or UnityPyBoost, defines and does not itself bind under that name; an alias of another library's object is not UnityPy's, and a name the defining module binds too is that module's (4) | `Module.name` in a code span: section 23 |
| `boost` | every public name of UnityPyBoost, and every public member of its classes, with a special method a class's C type defines (`__repr__`, `__del__`), the constructor's `__init__` and `__new__` aside (17) | `UnityPyBoost.name` in a code span, or a compact row: Appendix B |
| `enum_members` | every member of every enum UnityPy defines, `TextureFormat` aside, with its value (741) | a code span `` `NAME = value` `` in a column headed `values`, on the enum's row: Appendix C |
| `texture_formats` | every `TextureFormat` member (78) | a row of the section 17 matrix (the table whose header starts `value \| format`) whose `format` cell is `` `Name` `` and whose `value` cell is its value; a row of any other table names no format, and a second table with the matrix's header fails the check, as do two matrix rows with one format name or one value |

**Whole chains.** A dotted name counts only when it is written from the start of its chain. `Wrong.ImportHelper.check_file_type` does not name `ImportHelper.check_file_type`; the only prefixes allowed are the symbol's own import path (`UnityPy.helpers.ImportHelper.check_file_type`) or a package path that re-exports it (`UnityPy.classes.GameObject.m_Name`), and the only suffixes are what follows the chain (a call, a subscript, a further `.attribute`).

**Compact rows.** In a table with a column headed `fields`, `members` or `attributes`, a row whose first cell's first code span is `` `Parent` `` names `Parent.x` for every code span `` `x` `` in that column, and nothing under any other parent. In a column headed `values`, a code span `` `NAME = value` `` names `Parent.NAME` with that value.

**Out of scope.** Private names (a leading underscore) are not inventoried, special methods aside (the `methods` and `boost` groups). Nor is an attribute that only code outside its class sets (`obj.x = ...` in another module, or `setattr`), because the collector reads a class's own body and methods; an attribute a caller can see on such an object belongs to the row that sets it. The generated classes' attributes are their attrs fields: the one `legacy_patch` function that assigns through `self` (`Texture2D.set_image`) assigns only fields.

`check` exits 1 and lists what is missing. `tools/parity/tests/test_inventory.py` runs it against this file in the oracle image, requires it to fail when one entry of any group is removed or a name is written under a wrong parent, and tests the matching rules with the standard library alone; `tools/parity/tests/test_texture_matrix.py` checks the TextureFormat matrix against the oracle (section 17). Section 23 holds the module-level data that no other section names.

### Reproducing the numbers

The group sizes above come from `scripts/dev python tools/parity/inventory.py list --counts` in the oracle image, and `test_inventory.py` collect_finds_the_surface requires `collect()` to find exactly these sizes, so a change to UnityPy's surface or to the collector has to be made here too. The 869 generated classes, and the 869 rows of Appendix A, are counted by `test_inventory.py` appendix_a_has_one_row_per_generated_class. Appendix C's rows are what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/inventory.py enum-table` prints. The tables of sections 15.8 and 18.1, with their counts, and section 1's row for the libraries the Pillow writers call, with the versions of those the wheel bundles, are what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py writers`, `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py packers` and `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py libraries` print. `test_pillow_tables.py` packers_table_is_what_pillow_does, writers_table_is_what_pillow_does and libraries_row_is_what_the_writers_call hold them to it. Section 1's zlib in the oracle image, zlib 1.2.13, is what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/pillow_tables.py host` prints and `oracle_env.py`'s `ORACLE_IMAGE_ZLIB`: `test_oracle_env.py` the_image_zlib holds this file and `DESIGN.md` to that constant and, run outside a venv as in the image, checks that the process loads it, as `scripts/dev python tools/parity/oracle_env.py --image` does. In CI's venv nothing checks the runner's zlib. The tables of section 9.2.1, with their counts, are what `scripts/dev python tools/parity/object_mode.py` prints, and `test_object_mode.py` table_is_what_parse_class_does holds them to it. The Tier B count of section 17 (21) is pinned by `test_texture_matrix.py` tier_b_has_the_21_formats_design_names, and the example values of section 17.1 by `test_texture_matrix.py` tier_b_references_hold_their_stated_values and bc6h_reference_is_the_specification, and the departures of section 19.2's BC6H decoders by `test_texture_matrix.py` bc6h_libraries_depart_from_the_specification. The examples of section 13's negative sizes and positions are asserted by `test_rows_containers.py` reads_past_the_end_return_short_data; section 15.5's 2130706432-slot table, `int()` lines and negative offsets by `test_rows_exports.py` shader_text; and section 20's block counts, and which of them 1.25.2 reads as no entries or fails on, by `test_rows_writing.py` head_saves.

The shader numbers of section 19.1 (ShaderConverter fails from 2021.3.10 and on 6000.3) come from `scripts/dev python tools/parity/shader_survey.py --index /corpus/index.json --build 4100,100056`, which prints them as JSON; `--build 4100` or `--build 100056` gives one build's share.

## 23. Module-level data

Public tables, registries, settings and caches that no other section names (section 21, group `data`), the two functions a module-level loop leaves bound (`EndianBinaryReader.memory_read_func` and `EndianBinaryReader.stream_read_func`, group `functions`), and every alias (group `aliases`). Those named elsewhere: `config.FALLBACK_UNITY_VERSION` and `config.SERIALIZED_FILE_PARSE_TYPETREE` (sections 10 and 7.2), `environment.reSplit` (3), the `CompressionHelper` tables and magics (6), `ArchiveStorageManager.DECRYPT_KEY` and `ArchiveStorageManager.UNITY3D_SIGNATURE` (12), `Texture2DConverter.CONV_TABLE`, `Texture2DConverter.TEXTURE_FORMAT_BLOCK_SIZE_TABLE`, `Texture2DConverter.RG_PADDING_MAP` and `Texture2DConverter.XBOX_SWAP_FORMATS` (15.1, 17), `TextureSwizzler.TEXTURE_FORMAT_BLOCK_SIZE_MAP` (17), `GraphicsFormat.GRAPHICS_TO_TEXTURE_MAP` (14.2), the `VertexFormat` struct maps (15.3), `ShaderConverter.HEADER` (15.5), `Audio.AUDIO_TYPE_EXTEMSION` (16), `EndianBinaryReader.reNot0` (13), `extractor.EXPORT_TYPES` and `extractor.MONOBEHAVIOUR_TYPETREES` (15.7). The mutable tables among them (`CONV_TABLE`, `EXPORT_TYPES`, the codec maps) are extension points a UnityPy user may patch; the Python bindings (P3) decide which to mirror.

| item | UnityPy 1.25.2 | tier | level | test | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Appendix B. Fields, members and attributes of UnityPy's own classes

The attrs fields, the public members and the other public attributes (`inventory.py` groups `fields`, `methods` and `attributes`) of the classes UnityPy defines outside `classes/generated.py` that no row above names one by one, and the members of UnityPyBoost's `TypeTreeNode` (group `boost`), which UnityPy's `TypeTreeNode` inherits when UnityPyBoost is present. An attribute is a name the class body assigns or annotates, a name its own methods assign through `self` (`Environment.files`, `SerializedFile.objects`, `BundleFile.dataflags`), or a namedtuple field. The tier and level are those of the class's row in the section given; a member or attribute listed under a base class (`EndianBinaryReader`, `File`) is inherited by its subclasses, which list only what they add.

## Appendix C. Enum members

Every member of every enum UnityPy defines, `TextureFormat` aside (section 17), with its value, in declaration order (`inventory.py` group `enum_members`). The tier and level are those of the enum's row in the sections above: callers and the canonical dump read these names and values (`ClassIDType` names the dump's types, and `BuildTarget.Switch` and `BuildTarget.XBOX360` gate texture decoding), so unityrs takes them as they are. `scripts/dev python tools/parity/inventory.py enum-table` prints the rows below, and `check` fails when a member is missing or has another value.
